## 2026-10-09 — Two-polarity readout tested; offset improves but precision remains unresolved

**Stage:** analog precision feasibility, before complete PLIF integration and full-chip layout. The preceding goal turn made progress through qualified PMOS geometry and original-float range/noise sensitivity. This turn implements a different physical sampling method and tests a direct-comparator hypothesis; both provide evidence that changes the next action. Original epoch58 full-float model remains reference; no QAT/digital fallback.

New `screen_gf180_charge_shared_chopped_readout.py` uses native parent-context extracted PF80/PF10 followers, TGs, MIM banks and actual inverter phases. Inputs are programmed through real writers and then float; input crossbar reverses polarity. Two actual sample capacitors store outputs, then actual TGs share charge. This is a one-shot two-phase sampling experiment, NOT a completed continuous chopper/filter. Combined interconnect is schematic; qualified children do not constitute a new combined layout. No arithmetic averaging or offset correction is applied to physical state.

First same-polarity charge sharing leaves approximately-625.281uV nominal/-616.411uV seed29 output pedestal despite input zero. Cross-connecting the second capacitor during sharing physically changes output to+1.503640uV nominal/+6.412520uV seed29, versus direct phase-A seed29 offset-779.343uV. Signed seed29 inputs+100/-100uV produce raw+97.102800/-84.277300uV; two-point gain0.9069005. This offset improvement is not a precision pass: seed29 zero input moves stored state-7.425080uV; positive stored state100uV falls to79.237600uV after reads. Actual gate-rezero switches worsen zero output to60.720000uV. Four storage banks with gate-rezero give22.270900uV zero output and6.394540uV state displacement, increasing capacitor resources without solving precision. Keep all variants/evidence; do not select by the smallest nominal offset alone.

New `screen_gf180_chopped_capture_noise.py` freezes the actual phase-A sample aperture on, ideal-drives input state ONLY for stationary small-signal noise, and includes native TG/MIM/PF/inverter circuits. Differential capture-aperture RMS78.396638uV1Hz-100MHz, direct follower114.985939uV. This is NOT final held-sample covariance, transient-noise qualification or measured chopping suppression. Nominal geometric9x7x7um MIM density2fF/um2 gives882fF and illustrative27C equilibrium sqrt(kT/C)68.54uV; actual extracted bank C and switched covariance differ. Offset cancellation does not establish random-noise cancellation. No sampled-noise/retention/PVT/RPEX/gate-leakage qualification.

New `replay_gf180_float_comparator_noise.py` repeats two full440x640 original-float records,25 actors,10updates and three seeds. Hypothetical independent noise affects spike/competition comparisons only; candidate state arithmetic, subtractive reset amplitude and adaptation-energy input remain ideal. Ordinary baseline input/target/prediction hashes reproduce prior replay. Separate `replay_gf180_float_comparator_zero_control.py` enters the custom forward with1e-30uV error and reproduces record0 baseline prediction hash exactly, checking that the copied float operation order has not changed the result in this control. With channel-oracle+/-0.3V scaling,15uV comparison-only noise still reduces mean IoU to about0.118437/0.113125, nearly the prior noisy-candidate0.118687/0.113357. Thus avoiding noisy state writeback ALONE does not solve the tested linear-range sensitivity. Two records/three seeds and oracle calibration do not establish accepted precision or full validation accuracy; no measured device distribution is assumed.

**Method decision:** do not lay out the one-shot sampler as the selected PLIF precision solution. Retain its reusable qualified children and diagnostics. Next examine physically range-coded/companded charge-domain updates with direct comparison in the encoded domain, preserving beta once/bin, contribution completion before threshold, subtractive reset and adaptation/inhibition/affine order. Review prior physical encoder/inverse/range-crossing failures before selecting the next bounded implementation, rather than returning to another uniform linear voltage readout. Broader independent nonzero channel calibration and rail checks remain required. No new technology/process choice or user decision is needed yet.

Failed reports/logs/decks/snapshots preserved. Five corrected physical diagnostics complete, including noise, DC transfer, nominal signed transient and seed29 untrimmed/trimmed no-event controls.
- Extracted paired continuous read noise99.342271uV RMS1Hz-100MHz. Ideal4us boxcar output10.943703uV; nominal integrator-to-buffer gain0.841141 implies conditional input proxy13.010541uV. This spectral calculation is NOT an implemented average or measured sampled-state error. Ideal4us CDS with2T separation gives9.172072uV output and remains insufficient evidence. Sampled/storage/source/clock/reference noise and PVT are excluded.
- Nominal buffered first positive/negative increments+16.771600/-16.417185uV retain signs. Seed29 no-event buffered offset752.635uV reduces to-0.915338uV initially/-0.901867uV late with actual clamp-reference adjustment-754.6uV. This is offline trim using an ideal precision reference, not a completed physical servo or mismatch population result. Central input1.35-1.95V sampled DC gain0.996484-0.997765; fixed local-linear residual111.202838uV remains, with upper-rail headroom limitations.
- New original-float software replays use25 canonical actors,440x640 frames,10x5ms updates, independent Gaussian candidate perturbations before threshold/reset, seeds1729-1731 and hypothetical+/-0.3V state range. Baseline IoU0.205726/0.198918 and input/target/prediction hashes reproduce previous replays. Layer-wide oracle scaling with3uV noise gives mean IoU0.187842/0.171070; per-channel oracle scaling gives0.210477/0.199659. At15uV, even per-channel gives0.118687/0.113357. Two records, three seeds and optimistic oracle ranges do NOT establish accepted precision or validation accuracy; measured continuous noise is not assumed to be this independent error distribution.
- Separate calibration-frame trial fixes channel ranges from record0 and replays record1. Unclipped3uV/15uV sensitivity means0.200013/0.113995, but946/1856 channels exceed calibration ranges and some calibration ranges are zero. The raw overflow ratio uses an epsilon denominator for zero ranges and is not a meaningful finite hardware range ratio. This single-record calibration is REJECTED as an implementable range solution; no clipping was simulated, and zero-range channels receive optimistically zero assigned noise.

**Method decision:** retain analog signed charge-per-event architecture and reuse qualified geometry. Prefer per-channel scaling for further work, with broader independent calibration, nonzero range floors and rail checks. Next implement and characterize chopped/auto-zeroed differential readout, including switch injection, settling, sampled noise and time/area cost, before increasing capacitor area or adding long averaging lanes. Primary reference: https://www.analog.com/en/resources/technical-articles/2018/05/25/03/08/to-chop-or-auto-zero-that-is-the-question.html . Chopping targets offset/low-frequency noise but introduces switching errors; it is a candidate, not a proven fix. If insufficient, compare physically realized companded/charge-domain computation that avoids demanding a precise buffered linear state voltage. No process swap or digital fallback selected.

## 2026-10-08 — Method change: non-destructive current sensing selected for investigation

Overall stage: analog block validation, before complete PLIF integration. The destructive charge-read/capture/inverse/rewrite path remains unaccepted; repeated switch tuning has not removed its millivolt errors. Suspend further dummy-sizing sweeps and prioritize a small resident sense transistor with shared column readout, keeping membrane charge in its capacitor. This is a selected experiment, not an accepted chip architecture.

New scripts/screen_gf180_nondestructive_current_read.py (source cbf0ccec1dba) completed matched 20ns/10ns nominal runs at three states with two reads each. artifacts/analog_asic/results/gf180_nondestructive_current_read_boundary.json audits every recorded hash and exact deck equality except tran. Maximum post-second-read displacement is 3.086uV relative to the already-written pre-read state; step dependence reaches approximately 0.875uV, so this is not a precision/noise qualification. Temporary read coupling is 1.47–8.11mV and initial write-release error remains 16.81–33.41mV. Read currents are monotonic at these three points, but nonlinear; no implemented decoder/calibration. This does not demonstrate accurate state initialization or complete neural updates.

Native NF0.5um/L1um and 7um MIM are schematic; write/select/park TGs and clock inverters use qualified cap-extracted components. Ideal shared 1.65V column clamp and zero-drain parking remain bench assumptions. Zero parking avoids the modeled continuous sense-channel DC path; leakage, noise, mismatch, PVT, finite array loading and standby power remain unqualified. No sense-cell layout or new physical acceptance. All launched runs completed.

Alternatives: (1) non-destructive current read with shared column electronics is the next candidate; (2) local switched-capacitor updates can apply learned beta once per bin, signed weighted accumulation, threshold and subtractive reset in place, but require area/noise evaluation; (3) calibrated destructive read/restore remains a fallback with nonlinear inverse and switch-error burden. Uncontrolled continuous-time leakage is not a substitute for the trained bin equation. A switched-capacitor neuromorphic implementation is documented at https://arxiv.org/abs/1412.3233; it establishes precedent, not feasibility for this population.

Next milestone: implement and characterize the sense cell and a real shared column frontend, reduce initial-write error, then integrate one complete analog PLIF neuron preserving the original float model equations. Evaluate model tolerance, 5ms population service and realistic area before full-chip assembly/layout. No QAT/digital fallback. Original-float accuracy, complete PLIF, full service and full-chip layout remain unproven; goal active/incomplete. No critical user decision is needed for this reversible investigation.

## 2026-10-08 — Status correction and article scope

The user requests a suitable ASIC implementation for an article; manufacturing readiness and perfect device behavior are not the target. The original full-float SNN remains the reference, with no QAT requirement or digital fallback. Research claims still require faithful neuron semantics, defensible accuracy and service estimates, and real connectivity for any layout described as implemented.

Current result: circuit experiments and reusable primitive layouts, not an end-to-end ASIC. The completed integrity audit examined 28 imported views: 13 passed full-cell DRC and exported-GDS connectivity checks; 15 did not. A separately corrected phase inverter passes those checks and brings the import inventory to 29. This is 14 passing views across the original audit and the separate correction, not a fresh 29-view audit. Two routed MIM banks pass the stronger audit. Small nominal writeback experiments show approximately 0.2–1.4% preservation loss for an intermediate candidate, with incomplete switching cases retained. They do not establish a complete PLIF, validation-set accuracy, or 5 ms service. An optimistic 25 us / eight-feedback-MIM serial-service scenario needs about 599.7 mm2 of feedback plates alone for the resident population; this is a conditional architecture warning, not a full-chip area estimate.

All launched simulations and the integrity audit are terminal; no simulator is running. First-order Gear variants aborted, and the native reset-resistor variant did not provide a selected remedy. Historical launch entries below are superseded by this status.

Next priority: resolve architecture-level service and density, establish a tolerable error envelope on the original float model, and implement a representative weighted-event PLIF macro including threshold and subtractive reset. Use that macro for simulation and layout evidence with explicit chip-level extrapolation. Repair only the physical views required by the selected macro. Repeated local precision refinements are subordinate to these article objectives. Complete PLIF, full-model accuracy, 5 ms service, full-chip assembly and article acceptance remain open.

A shared encoder/inverse prototype reuses the same actual mirror/OTA with two extracted output-mode TGs and held ENC load capacitance. ## 2026-10-07 — physical nonlinear encoder settling and corner boundary

The active reference remains the original best epoch58 full-float SNN, with QAT off. Individual layouts now include a seven-MOS PMOS-input OTA, grounded one-MOS bias reference, two-MOS complementary feedback and an alternative six-MOS mirrored-feedback macro. All pass separate Magic DRC0 / unique Netgen LVS / real GDS; this does not qualify their assembled layout. Floating-substrate/unused-rail and oversized-gate-route failures were repaired with distinct preserved views and source-hashed evidence.

Direct feedback, including CMP-to-SUM MIM compensation, does not settle adequately under either trapezoidal or Gear integration. Current mirrors isolate the nonlinear transistor drains; SUM now drives amplifier IN and physical ENC-to-SUM capacitance supplies fast negative feedback. With four extracted7um MIMs, nominal20us steps still have3.263mV late error. Nominal100us final-window error is3.453uV, peak-to-peak0.466uV, VDD energy2.017nJ; this excludes reference/control generation and state access. Final-window min/max measurements distinguish settling from a single favorable late sample.

Corner gain remains a serious constraint: central slopes TT27/FF85/SS0 are21.366/0.351/868.114mV per nA at1V span. FF settles to its own curve, but SS zero-return error remains21.699mV over the final window even after1ms. Diagnostic1.2V span reduces SS100us error to6.630mV and changes TT central gain to1.963mV/nA; it is not a selected calibration or precision solution. Fixed common transfer, asinh equivalence and per-corner ideal calibration must not be inferred. Prototype supply references/input currents remain ideal diagnostics.

All47 encoder experiments completed; inadequate functional results are retained. The boundary audits immutable source snapshots, decks/logs/models and nested physical view hashes. No physical inverse, integrated synaptic/state update, sampled noise, original-float accuracy, combined layout, RPEX, silicon gate-leakage or population5ms service has been qualified. Next: measured encoder noise and physical reference/calibration for near-zero gain/settling, then physical inverse and exact signed-charge/beta/reset/adaptation interface before float replay and full assembly expansion. Full-chip goal remains active; no acceptance. Evidence: `artifacts/analog_asic/results/gf180_nonlinear_encoder_boundary.json`.

Calibration/noise continuation: stationary official-model output noise at zero input is295.647uV RMS TT27,363.860uV FF85 and277.417uV SS0 over10Hz..100MHz; TT +/-1nA gives295.204/297.784uV and+200nA329.755uV. Reference/input source noise and switched sampling are excluded; ngspice frequency-dependent integrated input-referred noise must not be substituted for DC state error. Corner-specific ideal-span diagnostics now settle over100us: SS0 span1.3V central28.591mV/nA, final-window error4.742uV, peak-to-peak1.690uV and output noise282.750uV; FF85 span0.65V central9.433mV/nA, final-window error2.081uV, peak-to-peak0.153uV and noise324.389uV. FF negative endpoint still has257.3uV virtual-sum error; FF span0.6V rails with3.279V sum error despite monotonic output, so rejected. These span settings are diagnostic ideal references, not implemented calibration or common model transfer. Physical span generation/calibration and usable signed range remain next gates. All47 encoder DC/transient/noise cases terminal/completed and provenance verified; completion does not imply functional acceptance.

Design guidance: [TI LOG112](https://www.ti.com/lit/ds/symlink/log112.pdf) discusses current-dependent compensation; [Analog Devices capacitive loading guidance](https://www.analog.com/en/resources/analog-dialogue/articles/techniques-to-avoid-instability-capacitive-loading.html) discusses feedback instability. These are design references, not qualification of the GF180 implementation.

The physical sampled10µm MIM weight level then moved+18.50mV over the5ms bin while driving that OTA input; the continuously biased buffer draws about8.9µA and follows the drift. Do not select this unrefreshed weight store. Explore static MOS-geometry encoded trained weights as a way to remove weight-refresh service: at one SS/0°C bias a0.5/1/2 width factor gives2.433/3.914/6.757µV signed charge equivalent, monotonic but nonlinear. Geometry range, sign symmetry, mismatch, PVT and whole-float weight/error coverage are unknown. Resident membrane storage remains an independent blocker. Reports are in `docs/RESULTS.md`.

The next bounded screen extends the static device-geometry candidate to five ideal shared analog gate biases. Under ideal continuous widths and independently chosen scales, three frozen-input full-float stages improve from6.85/3.33/2.84% best local spike mismatch with one bias to1.35/0.0003/0% with five. This is a sensitivity upper bound, not a selected circuit: the low0.4/0.5 bias rails generate sub-microvolt packets, a hypothetical10µV contribution floor raises final-fusion mismatch to3.17%, and the new SS/TT/FF corner screen shows about5.5× response spread at two candidate settings. Signed same-sign sequences work in two nominal points, but nanovolt cancellation remains simulator-only. Keep this encoding provisional until physical bias generation/distribution and calibration, mismatch/noise, legal layout, and task replay pass. It does not relieve the independent membrane-state gate. See `sky130_ms_static_geometry_float_mapping_screen.json` and `docs/RESULTS.md`.

The compact8-bit shared R-2R screen now supports static voltage generation at the loaded PDK gate boundary after one per-rail code trim: residual voltage error is≤3.51mV at TT/27°C and≤4.84mV when TT-trimmed codes are reused at SS/0°C and FF/27°C. This is only the bias voltage, not event charge transfer. The full existing24-bit ladder timed out at the bounded operating-point limit, and the compact ladder omits resistor mismatch/layout. Continue with pulse-time gate settling and compare integrated event charge against ideal rails before accepting even the shared-reference submodule. Static geometry remains provisional and membrane state/readout/service remain independent blockers.

Pulse-time gate transfer does not meet that reference check yet: the compact R-2R causes+3.46/−8.26% event-charge error at the two measured settings, and the one-code event-calibration attempt hits transient timestep collapse. Do not promote the geometry-plus-R-2R candidate. Continue analog debugging with a slewed/locally buffered shared gate driver or compare switched-capacitor injection; retain the direct ideal-bias event data only as an optimistic reference. The task remains full-float analog, with resident state/readout/service gates open.

The requested five-level event sweep also exceeds the120s bound with the current ten-ladder transient deck. Narrow the next event work to a low-count batch that resolves gate drive, then expand after runtime and convergence are under control. This timeout is a test-size limitation, not a physical feasibility result.

The12-bit shared R-2R is now the leading bias-generation screen: static codes trim within0.183mV nominally and measured positive event gain stays within3.33% of ideal over five gate fractions at one geometry. Applying those measured per-bias gain ratios in the optimistic float replay barely moves final-fusion local spike mismatch (1.35223→1.35244%). Continue this analog path, but keep the synapse and macro unqualified: only one geometry was tested for the five-level event curve, correction was measured TT/27°C, negative polarity is still untested, and the frozen replay assumes continuous geometry. The next required transfer test is negative polarity and signed accumulation.

## Current ASIC target policy — 2026-09-21

## No universal injector trim or small-cap acceptance — 2026-09-26

Keep the20um direct differential MOS gate-amplitude injector as the provisional schematic lead, with no selected global gain trim. Gain0.965 gives0.81–0.96% error on one maximum-fanin location and8.41µV ordering spread, but misses a selected near-threshold event by44–51nV against a0.358nV float threshold margin. Gain0.986 brings that event within0.18% yet moves the maximum-fanin site to1.34–1.61% error and23.46µV order spread. A10um MIM pair is4× smaller geometrically but gives6.25–6.60% error and significant CM shift in the same event screen. An ideal1nS equal-node behavioral CM servo stabilizes baseline CM but grows event-order span to52.16µV. Thus neither one global trim nor direct capacitor shrink is accepted. Next map transfer along the measured CM/differential trajectory and design a physical (possibly shared/multiplexed) CM regulator; remeasure weighted sequences and local threshold margins. Evidence and scope are in `docs/RESULTS.md` and the tagged simulation JSONs. No QAT or digital path.

## Analog synapse decision review — 2026-09-24

Use a signed charge-per-presynaptic-event interface on the active SKY130 prototype. No physical charge-delivery implementation is accepted yet. The passive packet and single-ended rail-gated injectors compress at high fan-in; the explicit current-source/TG-steering differential attempt has excessive charge injection. The provisional schematic lead is direct differential MOS gate-amplitude injection into a matched20um MIM pair: one corrected370-event full-float fan-in screen at0.15V headroom and0.965 gate-target trim is within0.96% of the target across three orderings, with8.4uV forward/reverse span when serialized at15ns intervals. The5.535us event train is followed by a5ms hold. This is one nominal TT site with ideal gate drivers; readout/threshold/reset, DAC implementation, noise, mismatch, PVT, extraction and task-level validation remain open. The older weighted runs were invalidated for PWL timestamp collapse. Select no accepted module yet. Do not use pulse-density error feedback across semantic bins or future events. Weight determines a connection's contribution; it does not independently set presynaptic firing frequency. Internal subpulses are implementation phases, not extra network spikes, and must finish within the same update.

The primary analog reference is the full-float seed-17 EventSpikeSeg140k best-validation checkpoint from the 80-epoch training run: `artifacts/model_checkpoints/dsec_6class/EventSpikeSeg140k_seed17_best.pt`, epoch 58 / best epoch 58, SHA-256 `c934da40b0f65d2a63155cb89b663e06aebf527cea1db4b9902a5b53840457d7`. The epoch-80 checkpoint is retained as a comparison, not the primary reference, because its metadata says `is_best=false`. Neither checkpoint contains ASIC-QAT provenance fields; analog work does not require QAT or a quantized reference. Float32 storage also does not imply that analog hardware must attain float32 numerical accuracy; its acceptable error is to be established from float-model state, spike and output sensitivity.

At the update boundary, reproduce the float model's signed weighted sum, learned beta, threshold/reset, inhibition and adaptation order. For this model's ordinary PLIF path, the installed SpikingJelly configuration uses `decay_input=False`, so its charge equation is `candidate = beta * previous_post_reset_state + weighted_current`; beta multiplies the old state only. Apply beta exactly once, threshold after the bin's contributions, and preserve raw-event multiplicity at ingress. Do not add QAT conventions or use a quantized export in the analog reference path.

The bin-based contract means finite pulse duration is not automatically less faithful than a capacitor packet: integrated signed charge and completion before the 5 ms boundary are decisive. Charge delivery, saturation, uncommanded droop and order effects must still be measured. Preserve all digital/XL artifacts and keep their campaigns stopped; no new Markdown files or full-model SPICE campaign.

Measured follow-up (2026-09-24): corrected effective-weight scaling puts pulse-only serial fan-in at 6.58ms median for enc1 and 15.94ms for final_fuse; the MOS virtual-summing-node switched-cap path remains the active research direction. The corrected physical sequence supersedes the earlier state-init failure notes: extending init to 2.5µs without overlap yields at most 109µV initial error; a 5ms OTA-disconnected hold changes state by at most 0.247mV; and the physically initialized 1µm packet after that hold produces −41.0…−41.3µV / +41.1…+41.2µV for ±0.3V top-plate steps. The packet bottom and OTA must disconnect before the top plate returns to VCM, or the event charge is canceled. A physically initialized two-packet/reset-reuse screen then produced same-sign totals around ±82µV, mixed-sign residuals ≤0.43µV, ≤0.62µV error against the two-single-packet sum and ≤0.075µV `pn`/`np` order difference. The later 5ms-bin test places events at 0.5ms and 4.5ms: the first event increment changes by ≤0.56µV over the 4ms idle interval, but same-sign final totals are −65.3/+65.3µV versus an ±82.3µV short-gap reference, with up to 17.03µV accumulation error and 4.20µV `pn`/`np` difference. Two no-event reconnect windows shift the stored output by 15.67mV; matched simulator subtraction is not an on-chip correction. A post-packet OTA settle tail reduces first-packet hold drift to 0.56µV but increases sum error; OTA bias sleep lowers drift to 0.24µV but leaves a 14.64mV common shift; 0.5µs soft-start lowers drift to 0.10µV but still leaves 14.54mV shift and 16.90µV sum error. OTA-output precharge is unstable. The physically anchored full-float replay on three patches gives 5.325/3.947/2.605% final_fuse spike mismatch and 91.805/93.319/94.092% pixel argmax agreement at 0.15/0.30/0.60V headroom, still sensitivity only. These nominal TT tests do not qualify PVT, mismatch, extraction or model-level accuracy. Full-float only; no QAT or quantized reference participates. Next explore a circuit-level OTA reconnect method that removes the common kick.


## Full analog implementation delegation — 2026-09-23

The user now authorizes Grok to perform the full ASAP7 analog/mixed-signal workflow, including design, debug, per-module simulation, physical macro generation, full assembly and ORFS execution once readiness passes. `docs/ACTIVE_TASKS.md` is the authoritative staged task plan. Earlier Grok-test-only and blanket no-ORFS instructions are superseded. Keep digital/XL jobs paused and all artifacts preserved. Use existing docs and YAML only. Approximate research estimates are allowed with explicit evidence/coverage; full layout still requires real implemented macros and validated assembly.

## 2026-09-23 — Multiplexed channel uses digital membrane restore glue

Decision: retain the SKY130 analog PLIF + digital Q7.16 state experiment, but do not treat one/few physical channels as population-feasible. Restore uses `membrane_code_vdac_offset_u24` + TG write (`analog_plif_mux_channel`), with a signed range mapping and one-point TT trim. Ideal R and trim are provisional; measured restore error is 46.04 Q7.16 voltage LSBs at worst, so analog Q7.16 precision is unqualified. SAR writeback/phase RTL exists, but has only an ideal comparator code-level smoke and does not yet include the restore trim. The signed weight injector screen fails low-code response and sign-current matching. Servicing 30,976,000 identities in a 5ms update requires 743,424,000 comparisons; at 100MHz, at least 1,487 lanes before other phases. The shared-channel architecture is therefore gated on a viable analog transfer and parallelism/area plan. Evidence: `scripts/test_sky130_ms_restore_mux.py`, `scripts/tb_sky130_ms_slot_controller.sv`, `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_weight_q716_injector_screen.json`.

## 2026-09-23 — Stop digital ORFS; mixed-signal sole active path

Decision: on user instruction, stop and remove all live digital 140k ASAP7 hard-macro ORFS workers/containers. Do not delete digital artifacts (views, RTL, journal history). Mark in-flight journal masters `paused` with reason mixed-signal-only. `digital_or_XL_restart` remains false until explicit user request. Sole active implementation is SKY130 mixed-signal analog PLIF. Evidence: `artifacts/ASIC/digital_stop_20260923/`.

## 2026-09-23 — Mixed-signal analog PLIF on SKY130 (chosen implementation path)

Decision: implement the theoretical SNN as **mixed-signal**:
- **Analog PLIF channel (SKY130):** membrane on PDK MIM; synaptic multiply as spike-gated MOS current × pulse width; decay as MOS leak to vcm on an explicit phase; threshold via MOS comparator. Sources: `asic/analog/sky130_ms_plif.spice`. Bounded Docker smoke `python scripts/test_sky130_analog_plif.py` passes inject+5ms hold, leak-toward-vcm, and threshold→spike.
- **Digital:** routing, phases, weight/index delivery, and **membrane codes between analog updates** (ASAP7 digital campaign preserved/paused; SKY130 digital also available). Always-resident 31M MIMs remain area-infeasible; multiplexing physical analog channel(s) with digital state restore is the declared population mapping (logical inventory conserved; not a silent neuron-count cut).
- **ASAP7:** keep for digital PPA / prior schematic smoke only. Open ASAP7 failed analog storage (no MIM/MOM; MOS-Cgg leak). Do not claim ASAP7 analog membrane qualification.

## 2026-09-23 — Analog physical storage pivots to SKY130 MIM (ASAP7 MOS-Cgg closed)

Decision: stop ASAP7 same-family MOS-Cgg sweeps. Bounded nMOS/pMOS screens and IGTOT show gate leakage cannot meet the provisional 5 ms hold budget across the needed state range without inventing compensation (~48 ppm) or capacitor physics; ASAP7 open PDK has no MIM/MOM. Do not pursue ideal leakage servos or a powered OTA integrator as the first storage substitute (different semantics; servo accuracy is unrealizable as an “ideal source”).

Next analog path: SkyWater SKY130 PDK capacitor cells already licensed in-tree (`cap_mim_m3`, VPP). First Docker MIM retention screen (`scripts/test_sky130_vpp_storage.py`): `sky130_fd_pr__cap_mim_m3_2` at w=l=100 µm (~20 pF at camimc=2e-15 F/µm²) with TG holds 5 ms within the provisional 0.02-unit limit at 0.2 V/unit. Preserve ASAP7 schematic smoke and digital/XL artifacts as secondary/reference; do not mix SKY130 physical views into ASAP7 ORFS.

Immediate follow-on: drain/adjust MIM bench, then population area gate (≈10⁴ µm²/membrane at 20 pF ⇒ ~3×10⁵ mm² for 30.976M membranes) before any layout campaign.

## 2026-09-23 — ASAP7 capacitor path Option B (MOSFET Cgg storage)

Decision: proceed with Option B. Membrane storage uses ASAP7 FinFET gate capacitance (MOSCAP: gate = state, drain/source tied to vcm) so charge can be drained through a reset transmission gate and adjusted through write/inject switches. Open ASAP7 PDK has no MIM/MOM device card; this is an explicit physical approximation, not a discrete capacitor qualification. Instance order is drain-gate-source-bulk (same as inverter benches). Characterize C(V) with leakage-subtracted |i|/(dv/dt), require 5 ms retention for Option-B acceptance, and keep readiness content-validated. Bounded Docker tests only before layout. Digital/XL remain paused.

## 2026-09-23 — Freeze ASAP7 analog Stage 2 contracts from quantized 140k export

Decision: treat inspected quantized export ranges and sampled 5 ms phase order as the sizing interface for ASAP7 analog work. Record VDD=0.7 V, vcm=0.35 V, and smoke 0.07 V/state-unit as provisional encoding only; keep separate shared-cell variants because weight absmax reaches ≈68 while thresholds stay ≈0.29..0.77. Label 0.02 as circuit smoke tolerance only. Keep logistic compare, finite-gm sense, ideal capacitors and B-source synapses as explicit pending physical work. Do not claim physical PLIF or ORFS readiness from Stage 2.


## Active approach override — 2026-09-23

User authorized analog/mixed-signal 140k feasibility as the active approach. Follow docs/ARCHITECTURE.md and write outputs to artifacts/analog_asic. Digital-only/register-state rules below now describe the preserved digital fallback, not restrictions on this analog experiment. Analog approximations and scaled estimates are allowed with explicit limitations; distinguish ideal, schematic, extracted and full-chip evidence. Preserve all digital/XL artifacts and keep their jobs paused. Prior active work is in docs/PROGRESS.md.


All primary ASIC layout, block simulation, power, area, timing, and inventory work is based on **EventSpikeSeg140k v8, epoch 80, seed 17**: six classes, 440x640, 25 permanent PLIF actors, 54 graph edges, 30,976,000 resident PLIF identities, and 143,824 deployed synaptic weights. Primary exports/results live under `artifacts/ASIC/`; the latest accepted small gate-simulation results are in `artifacts/ASIC/primitive_characterization/`.

**EventSpikeSegXL is secondary/reference work.** Preserve useful accepted XL GDS/LEF/Liberty views, final routed ODB/DEF/netlist/SDC/SPEF files, checkpoint-specific constants, hierarchy catalogs/instance maps/preflight, and validation/power reports. Keep them under `artifacts/ASIC_XL/` and their existing named OpenROAD result directories. Retain the latest useful unfinished-run database and failure summaries; redundant intermediate databases and rebuildable simulation binaries need not be retained. Never relabel XL measurements or its 44-actor inventory as 140k results. Reuse an XL physical view for 140k only after proving matching RTL, constants, geometry, interfaces and constraints.

XL physical expansion remains paused behind 140k work. Preserve independent XL training and checkpoints. Neither model has a completed full-chip layout; existing routed blocks and structural hierarchy data must be labeled accordingly. Current work uses small per-block simulations and extracted-layout measurements; whole-model simulation remains retired. Historical entries below retain their original model and date and do not change this priority.

## 2026-09-07 — Bound validation with the configured temporal chunk count

Use `--sequential-chunks` for validation as well as the memory-bounded training replay.
Validation remains under `no_grad`, carries recurrent state chronologically between
chunks, and merges chunk logits/rates into one complete-record prediction. This prevents
the full ten-update validation forward from exceeding a training run deliberately sized
with smaller temporal chunks; it does not introduce partial-history validation metrics.

## 2026-09-07 — Schedule final-model loss by completed epoch fraction

Use one direct `progress = current_epoch / --epochs` loss calculation for both
`EventSpikeSeg140k` and `EventSpikeSegXL`, with no phase table or anchor lookup. Preserve
a total CE coefficient of 1.0 using `macro=0.45+0.25*progress` and
`pixel=0.55-0.25*progress`. Calculate Dice as `0.10+0.55*progress`, boundary BCE as
`0.02*progress`, and auxiliary CE as `0.20-0.15*progress`.
Keep focal, static class weights, and neighbor consistency disabled. XL's deep auxiliary
CE remains one half of the scheduled mid auxiliary coefficient. Save the active loss
profile in every checkpoint and report it in each epoch summary.

## 2026-09-07 — Expand XL below one million parameters without widening native tensors

Increase the software-only `EventSpikeSegXL` from roughly 236k to 978,559 parameters
for six classes and 979,179 for eleven. Keep the 32/64/128 widths so the already
memory-bound native-resolution activations do not grow. Use full 3x3 spiking
convolutions in the deepest encoder refinement, both serial context blocks, two new
quarter-resolution capacity blocks, and both decoder fusion points. Remove the binary
96-channel selector before final fusion because it can suppress weak evidence; the full
3x3 fusion now learns spatial and channel selection jointly. This is a capacity
experiment, not evidence or a guarantee of 50% mIoU, and it does not change the frozen
140k ASIC graph. Use the same continuous temporal class-current readout as the 140k
model so ten class-spike counts do not quantize away final evidence magnitude. Balance
localization and state by retaining a multi-stage 16-channel native detail path and a
24-channel native final refinement, while placing the 32-channel semantic stem at
220x320 and the added full-convolution capacity at 55x80. This reduces inference
membranes from 215,705,600 for the wide-everywhere layout to 78,707,200 without relying
on the 55x80 representation alone for small-object localization.

## 2026-09-06 — Add a checkpoint-compatible no-custom-mechanism ablation

Test the completed epoch-80 140k model without the mechanisms formerly described
as FInST/FLong by forcing peer-membrane inhibition and triangular threshold
adaptation to zero. Do not physically delete PLIF stages or trained structural
blocks: that would invalidate checkpoint keys and mix a structural ablation with
the neuron-mechanism question. Load epoch-80 weights only, use a fresh AdamW
optimizer at 1e-5 for exactly one epoch, retain the normal sequential half-stream
sampling, and write into a separate `no_custom_mechanisms` checkpoint directory.

## 2026-09-05 — Make resume LR an explicit absolute override

Replace the ambiguous `--resume-max-lr` ceiling with `--resume-lr`, an explicit
override for the largest initial optimizer-group LR. Preserve initial group
ratios, retain the checkpoint's completed epoch, set the cosine horizon from the
current `--epochs`, and recompute the current LR at that epoch. For epoch 74 of
80, an initial LR of 1e-3 therefore resumes near 1.38e-5 instead of forcing the
current LR to 1e-3. A one-epoch ablation checkpoint continued to epoch 80 now
uses epoch 1 of an 80-epoch cosine curve instead of restoring its exhausted
one-epoch curve at zero LR.

## 2026-09-03 — Balance workers and two-record prefetch

Retain the intentional `prefetch_factor=2` throughput setting, reduce the DSEC
Compose service from eight workers to four, and retain the 512 MiB container
`/dev/shm` limit. This permits eight queued native 440x640 records, preserving CPU
preprocessing overlap without the sixteen-record pressure that exhausted shared
memory. Report the factor consistently in startup output and checkpoint metadata.

## 2026-09-03 — Restore rare-class CE and delay backbone refinement

The epoch-40 to epoch-60 checkpoint comparison showed that the six-class model
retained useful IoU for broad classes but produced effectively zero object and
human IoU. The decoder-detail phase beginning at 70% did not recover either class
and held mIoU near 25.8%, below the 27.44% epoch-40 best. Restore the intended
0.5 pixel-mean CE plus 0.5 target-present-class macro CE, increase present-class
Dice moderately from 0.3 to 0.4, retain auxiliary CE at 0.1, and reduce boundary
BCE from 0.02 to 0.01. Keep all semantic-backbone modules trainable through 90%
of training and reserve decoder/detail-only refinement for the final 10%.

## 2026-08-31 — Simplify the active DSEC segmentation objective

Replace the final-run 0.5 pixel CE + 0.5 target-present macro CE + 0.3 Dice + 0.05 same-class region-consistency objective with a literature-aligned segmentation loss: 1.0 ordinary pixel CE + 0.3 Dice averaged over target-present classes + 0.10 auxiliary semantic CE + 0.02 boundary BCE. Keep the SNN activity regularizer because it constrains spike dynamics rather than semantic class weighting. Remove the macro-CE contribution and region-consistency contribution from the active final run. Historical benchmark suites retain their frozen loss profiles.

# Decisions

## 2026-09-23 — MOSCAP retention gate fails; do not scale

Keep MOSFET Cgg as an unqualified experiment only. The corrected bounded 8-finger test completed but lost 0.197 V over 5 ms (2.816 model units), versus the provisional 0.02-unit limit. Drain/adjust passing does not change this outcome. Do not size a population or create layouts from the measured Ceff until the hold/leakage path is redesigned or a different storage structure is supported and passes the retention gate. The candidate flag remains false and full ORFS readiness remains false.

Isolation found no passing MOS Cgg alternative among the tested bounded structures: nMOS 8/32 fingers fail; pMOS 8 fingers improves but still fails, and larger pMOS counts worsen drift. Ideal-cap controls show the same transmission gate itself requires much larger C for this 5ms hold (1pF still fails the provisional model-unit tolerance; 20pF passes only as an idealized control). The open ASAP7 flow supplies no qualified physical capacitor view. The user directed that analog exploration continue and digital resume only on explicit instruction. Continue bounded analog diagnosis without scaling/layout. Direct BSIM-CMG `IGTOT` confirms pMOS MOSCAP gate current is real in the current model; a low-common-mode nMOS-only screen reduced intrinsic leakage at its lowest state but failed at other states and through the access path. The installed ASAP7 collateral has no discrete capacitor device model/PCell. Keep all storage concepts unqualified until a complete analog hold/refresh cell passes its state/input retention criterion; do not switch to digital absent an explicit user request.

## 2026-09-07 — Finish final models with range-matched fixed-point QAT

Train both final architectures in FP32 for optimization stability, then enable ASIC
quantization-aware forward execution for the last three epochs by default. Keep FP32 master
parameters and straight-through gradients, but fake-quantize folded convolution/readout weights,
PLIF accumulator/membrane, beta, threshold, and retinotopic adaptation values on the exact export
grids. Use range-matched precision rather than int8: signed 24-bit Q7.16 weights and membrane/
threshold, signed 32-bit Q15.16 combined accumulator, unsigned 24-bit UQ1.23 beta, 19-bit UQ3.16
strength/amplitude, 21-bit UQ5.16 duration, and exact 6-bit integer age. Restart best-checkpoint
selection when QAT begins so the canonical checkpoint is always one validated with deployment
arithmetic. QAT overrides the earlier refinement/neuron freezes: all model parameters are
unfrozen so convolution, readout, beta, threshold, inhibition, and adaptation values can jointly
compensate for the deployment grid. `--asic-qat-epochs 0` remains an explicit diagnostic escape
hatch.

## 2026-09-02 — Spatial hierarchy must preserve dedicated PLIF state

Use spatial tiles as elaboration and physical hierarchy boundaries, not as time-multiplexed caches.
Each tile owns dedicated current/membrane/adaptation elements for its PLIFs and evaluates global
convolution coordinates. It may share immutable weight constants with peer tiles, but it must not
fetch or write runtime state through an addressed memory interface. Nominal 8x8 partitioning of
the checkpoint model creates 20,410 tiles while preserving 30,976,000 PLIFs and 1,590,476,800
resident state bits. Isolated tile synthesis can provide per-configuration evidence, but a scaled
sum is only an estimate until complete placement and routing includes clocking, pulse distribution,
completion trees, readout, and wiring.

## 2026-08-29 — Size Docker shared memory for eight DSEC workers

Keep the user-selected eight CPU preprocessing workers with one prefetched record per
worker and raise the DSEC container `/dev/shm` ceiling from 256 MiB to 512 MiB. Eight
native 440x640 records occupy about 190 MiB before PyTorch multiprocessing queue,
in-flight transfer, and pinned-host-copy overhead, which caused the observed bus error
and `No space left on device` at 256 MiB. The ceiling is backed on demand and does not
reserve VRAM.

## 2026-08-29 — Train connected semantic regions without inference post-processing

Keep final Dice at 0.3 and add a 0.05 target-aware four-neighbor consistency term
over the final softmax probabilities. Penalize class-distribution discontinuity only
between adjacent valid pixels whose ground-truth classes match; do not smooth across
true class boundaries or ignore-index borders. Reduce the training-only boundary-head
BCE from 0.05 to 0.02 because validation predictions remain excessively aligned to
sparse event edges. Reuse the Dice softmax and compute Dice per class without a dense
one-hot target to contain training memory. The new loss adds no inference parameters,
post-processing, latency, or FPGA hardware.

## 2026-08-27 — Make six broad DSEC classes the active 140k training and ASIC target

Replace the active eleven-output semantic head with six outputs while retaining the exact
v8 spike-preserving backbone and native 440x640 input. Remap source labels as follows:
`road|sidewalk -> flat`, `sky|building|fence|wall -> construction+sky`,
`pole|traffic-sign -> object`, `vegetation -> nature`, `person -> human`, and
`vehicle -> vehicle`. The active readout is therefore 16->6, with 149,835 registered
parameters, 149,423 executed inference parameters, and 143,824 executed synaptic weights.
The 25 PLIF populations and their 30,976,000 logical membrane states do not change.

Give the six-class data contract its own protocol version and checkpoint directory so an
eleven-class checkpoint cannot be partially resumed. Preserve `--dsec-classes 11` only
for reproducibility; the active Docker training service and ASIC exporter require six.

## 2026-08-05 — Reset from MEVDT tracking to DDD17 segmentation

Tracking, box decoding, alpha-beta tracking, global classification, and MEVDT metrics are inactive. The task is six-class semantic segmentation using the Ev-SegNet DDD17 representation.

## 2026-08-05 — Preserve the six event-statistic channels

The six channels are polarity histogram, timestamp mean, and timestamp standard deviation pairs. They are repeated as direct current over internal SNN steps and are not reinterpreted as chronological bins.

## 2026-08-06 — Preserve the completed ten-model experiment separately

Round one completed with `v10_fpga_hybrid` highest at mIoU 0.3130. The complete preset registry is moved to `top_ten_models.py`; it remains reproducible but is no longer part of the canonical production module.

## 2026-08-06 — Keep model.py canonical-only

`model.py` contains one architecture interface. It currently installs the measured round-one incumbent. Experimental architectures belong in explicit experiment files and may not be added back to `model.py`.

## 2026-08-06 — Run a spike-native top-five round

The second round tests combinations derived from v10, v03, v02, and v08 using PLIF, feature-map threshold adaptation with an explicit duration, and selected hidden-layer inhibition. Ordinary BN and GAP gating are not included because this round targets mechanisms that remain meaningful during neuromorphic inference.

## 2026-08-06 — Do not enable hidden inhibition in every contender

Cross-channel winner-take-all may improve sparsity but can remove simultaneous semantic features. It is therefore isolated to two of five contenders rather than treated as automatically beneficial.

## 2026-08-06 — Treat PLIF as trainable, not yet hardware-exact

PLIF learns continuous per-channel retention. The eventual FPGA model must quantize it to an integer-friendly leak, then run accuracy and bit-exact regression tests before PLIF can be claimed as deployed.

## 2026-08-06 — Round-one zero-IoU classes are an open data/training issue

Object and human class performance cannot be solved by architecture ranking alone. Before final selection, record class pixel counts, image-presence counts, prediction counts, and confusion matrices at the benchmark resolution.

## 2026-08-07 — Reject the first PLIF/adaptation top five as final models

The first round-two winner reached only mIoU 0.2464 versus the incumbent 0.3130, and the lightweight `f04` emitted zero output spikes. Preserve the definitions/checkpoints for diagnosis but do not promote them.

## 2026-08-07 — Replace fixed adaptation hold with learnable continuous persistence

A discrete integer hold timer cannot receive useful gradients through standard backpropagation. Revised rate adaptation learns a bounded continuous `tau`, with retention `exp(-1/tau)`, plus per-channel adaptation strength and target firing rate. Quantize these only after architecture selection.

## 2026-08-07 — Make adaptation bidirectional and rate-based

Use spatial mean spike rate instead of feature-map `amax`. Threshold adaptation may become negative for silent channels and positive for overactive channels, bounded around the learnable base threshold.

## 2026-08-07 — Remove hard class-head WTA from revised contenders

Independent output class neurons accumulate spikes and compete only through final temporal argmax. This preserves a fully spiking learned output while allowing gradients to reach rare classes.

## 2026-08-07 — Restrict hidden inhibition rather than apply it globally

Only grouped top-2 competition is allowed, and only in the encoder or stem of selected contenders. Global one-winner competition caused severe information loss and dead-layer behavior.

## 2026-08-07 — Separate revised results from failed top-five results

This rerun was temporarily separated from the failed first attempt for diagnosis. That versioned active-directory convention is now retired: the completed structural five lives in `artifacts/ablation/top_five/` and the active three lives in `artifacts/ablation/top_three/`.

## 2026-08-07 — Use the completed mechanism screen to freeze neuron dynamics for structural search

The completed five-model screen used seed 17, 5 epochs, 256 training samples, 128 held-out samples, 64×64 output, and 8 internal timesteps. `f05_v02_light_peer_membrane_inhibition` ranked first at mIoU 0.1991. `f02` threshold adaptation alone ranked second at 0.1459, while hidden `winner_peer` inhibition ranked last at 0.1233 and drove `enc3` spike rate to approximately 0.00015.

Decision: the next top-five holds neuron dynamics constant as PLIF + learnable threshold + stem-only `peer_membrane` inhibition + encoder triangular threshold adaptation. Keep affine scaling, residual paths, auxiliary supervision, and semantic output class WTA. Do not use hidden `winner_peer` gating in the active five.

Reason: this separates the next question—network width, receptive field, depth, and context—from the already-screened inhibition question. The low-budget run is sufficient for mechanism selection but not for promotion accuracy claims.

## 2026-08-07 — Make width, early kernel size, and encoder depth explicit architecture variables

Add explicit channel schedules, configurable stem/first-block kernel sizes, and optional fourth encoder scale to the shared segmentation backbone. Preserve the original three-scale module layout for the canonical/top-ten models.

The active five are:

- `f01_base24_multiscale`: 24/48/96, 3×3, multiscale context
- `f02_wide32_multiscale`: 32/64/128, 3×3, multiscale context
- `f03_early5x5_multiscale`: 24/48/96, 5×5 stem and first depthwise block
- `f04_deep4_dilated`: 24/48/96/128, four encoder scales, single dilated context
- `f05_compact16_dilated`: 16/32/64, single dilated context

This replaced the previous top-five definitions. `TOP_FIVE_PRESETS` is now the single completed structural registry; the active search moved to `TOP_THREE_PRESETS`.

## 2026-08-08 — Complete the structural top-five and move the search toward feature preservation

The fixed-dynamics structural benchmark completed with seed 17, 8 epochs, 2,048 training samples, 512 held-out samples, 64×64 output, 15 internal timesteps, batch 14, and learning rate 0.002.

Measured ranking:

- `f05_compact16_dilated`: mIoU 0.2596, 21,491 parameters
- `f02_wide32_multiscale`: mIoU 0.2284, 77,751 parameters
- `f04_deep4_dilated`: mIoU 0.2079, 107,131 parameters
- `f01_base24_multiscale`: mIoU 0.1626, 46,423 parameters
- `f03_early5x5_multiscale`: mIoU 0.1482, 49,111 parameters

Decision: do not promote any of these models. `v10_fpga_hybrid` remains the 0.3130 mIoU incumbent.

Findings used for the next round:

- compact `16/32/64` + a single dilated context gives the strongest overall accuracy/parameter tradeoff, so more parameters alone are not the answer;
- moderate width helped feature diversity and produced the best object IoU in the round (`0.0141`);
- the four-stage model produced the best aggregate rare-class signal, especially nature (`0.0712`) and vehicle (`0.1230`), so the deep encoder is extracting useful semantics that are being lost during decoding;
- early 5×5 processing is rejected;
- averaged multiscale branches are rejected for the next round because they erase branch identity and underperformed the simpler single-dilation path.

## 2026-08-08 — Create a separate top-three accuracy search

Create `top_three_models.py` instead of modifying the canonical model or the completed top-five registry. The three models isolate three accuracy hypotheses:

- `t01_spike_pyramid_concat`: preserve dilation-1/2/4 branch identity with channel concatenation, binary spike-count channel selection, and a spiking 1×1 fusion; use concatenative decoder skip fusion.
- `t02_dualpath_detail`: retain the compact semantic path but add a narrow persistent high-resolution spiking detail stream for small classes and boundaries.
- `t03_deep_attention_fpn`: retain four-stage semantic depth, add low-resolution spike-driven binary-mask attention, and use a concatenative FPN decoder to restore shallow spatial information.

The parameter targets are approximately 63k, 24k, and 109k respectively. Around 100k parameters is acceptable for an accuracy candidate, but fewer parameters win when validation accuracy is effectively equivalent.

## 2026-08-08 — Keep attention spike-oriented

Do not introduce conventional dense softmax self-attention into the active FPGA/neuromorphic search. The top-three may use:

- spike-count channel selection with a hard binary forward gate;
- low-resolution Q/K binary coincidence, channel accumulation, learned comparison threshold, and V masking;
- concatenation followed by spiking 1×1 mixing.

This is informed by SLTNet's evidence for multi-receptive-field event features, channel interaction, multi-level decoder restoration, and spike-driven masked attention, but the project tests smaller hardware-oriented modules rather than reproducing the full SLTNet network.


## 2026-08-09 — Complete the top-three search and select the dual-path mechanism

The accuracy-oriented top-three benchmark completed at seed 17 with 8 epochs, 2,048 training samples, 512 held-out samples, 64×64 output, and 15 internal timesteps. Results were `t02_dualpath_detail` mIoU 0.2728 / 24,211 parameters, `t01_spike_pyramid_concat` 0.1671 / 63,147, and `t03_deep_attention_fpn` 0.1555 / 108,781. T2 therefore provides the strongest new structural evidence: preserve a persistent high-resolution detail stream and fuse semantic + shallow + detail spikes late. T3's extra depth/attention is rejected for the canonical model because its predictions collapsed toward the two dominant classes.

## 2026-08-09 — Install the initial scaled spike-native dual-path candidate (later extended)

Replace the old `v10_fpga_hybrid` architecture in `model.py` with `final_dualpath_wide32`, while preserving `v10` in the frozen top-ten registry as the measured 0.3130 mIoU incumbent. The new candidate uses a 32/64/128 three-scale semantic encoder, extra residual depthwise/pointwise refinement per scale, a persistent 32-channel high-resolution detail path, two serial dilation-2 context blocks, a stronger decoder, and late semantic/shallow/detail concatenation. At this revision, do not add a fourth spatial scale, early 5×5 processing, averaged parallel multiscale context, dense softmax attention, or the failed T3 attention architecture wholesale. This restriction is later refined: one bottlenecked low-resolution residual spike-mask attention block is reintroduced as an additive feature, without the fourth T3 scale/FPN design. The candidate is not yet an accuracy claim; it requires larger-budget and multi-seed validation.

## 2026-08-09 — Try comparator-only spike-count selection and independent class spikes (selector later superseded)

For the canonical circuit-oriented path, replace the top-three count-domain MLP channel selector with one learned activity threshold per fused channel. Inference is spike counting, comparison, and binary AND gating only. Also disable hard per-timestep semantic class WTA in the canonical head: six independent spiking class populations accumulate evidence and final segmentation uses temporal spike-count argmax. This remains fully spiking while avoiding early suppression of rare class evidence. Historical benchmark definitions remain unchanged.

## 2026-08-09 — Use SpikingJelly as the PLIF/convolution infrastructure layer

Keep PyTorch as the tensor/autograd/optimizer substrate, but require SpikingJelly public `activation_based` components for learned synaptic stages. `SpikingStage` uses the SpikingJelly `Conv2d` synapse layer, and `ParametricLIF` delegates its charge equation to SpikingJelly `ParametricLIFNode`. Preserve the already-benchmarked per-channel beta by feeding a per-channel reciprocal-tau tensor into the SpikingJelly PLIF charge equation. Custom dynamic threshold, peer-membrane inhibition, triangular threshold adaptation, reset ordering, and hardware-visible state remain explicit project code. There is no conventional PyTorch Conv2d model fallback.

## 2026-08-09 — Add late decoder/detail refinement training

The canonical model trains all layers jointly through 70% of epochs. From 70% to 90%, freeze only the semantic backbone (stem, encoder, and semantic context) while keeping the high-resolution detail path, decoder/fusion, auxiliary head, and class head trainable; auxiliary CE/focal coefficients rise from 0.15 to 0.30 during this phase. At 90%, unfreeze all layers and apply an additional 0.25 learning-rate multiplier on top of the existing cosine decay for final joint tuning. Completed benchmark models have no refinement schedule and retain their original training behavior. The main segmentation CE coefficient is 1.0.


## 2026-08-09 — Replace comparator-only final selector with PLIF cross-channel selector

The comparator-only selector was more circuit-minimal but discarded T2's learned cross-channel relationships. The canonical `final_dualpath_wide32` therefore uses a spike-driven 96→96 1×1 synaptic projection followed by PLIF selector neurons. Selector spikes are reduced to binary per-channel enables and gate the fused semantic/shallow/detail spikes. This preserves cross-channel learned selection while keeping inference spike-native: active input spikes cause stored 1×1 weights to be accumulated into PLIF membranes, followed by threshold/fire gating. No dense count-domain MLP or softmax is used. Historical T2 remains unchanged for reproducibility. The canonical model is now approximately 141k trainable parameters.


## 2026-08-09 — Scale the canonical model by additive spike-native features

Decision: expand `final_dualpath_wide32` from the ~141k revision to 237,597 trainable parameters. Hardware simplicity alone is no longer a reason to remove an accuracy-oriented feature; features are excluded only when prior ablations give meaningful negative evidence or when they require a fundamentally dense/non-spiking inference path.

Added:
- projected cross-scale PLIF residual bridges (`32->64`, `64->128`) to reduce information loss through strided thresholded stages;
- residual parallel d1/d2/d4 depthwise context while retaining the existing serial d2 context;
- one low-resolution bottlenecked spike-mask attention residual;
- concatenative 64+64 mid-decoder fusion;
- asymmetric spike-native detail refinement;
- a second deep auxiliary head used only during training.

Retained unchanged:
- 32/64/128 three-scale semantic backbone;
- persistent 32-channel high-resolution detail path;
- PLIF, learnable threshold, stem-only peer-membrane inhibition, and encoder/context triangular threshold adaptation;
- late semantic+shallow+detail concatenation;
- 96->96 PLIF cross-channel selector;
- independent class spiking neurons with temporal count decision;
- 70/90% freeze/refinement schedule and CE coefficient 1.0.

The deep auxiliary loss is intentionally weaker than the mid auxiliary loss: 0.10 CE + 0.10 focal normally and 0.20 + 0.20 during decoder/detail refinement.


## 2026-08-09 — Consolidate all architecture suites into one trainer

Remove the standalone top-three training implementation. `train.py` is the single engine for `top-ten`, `top-five`, `top-three`, and `final`, while `top_*_models.py` contain definitions only. Preserve historical reproducibility by pinning top-ten/top-five/top-three to the recorded `0.5 CE + 0.5 focal + 0.75 Dice` objective and historical test-split evaluation. Keep the final suite on CE `1.0`, training-split validation holdout, per-epoch best-model selection, periodic checkpoints, and the 70/90% refinement schedule.

The active evaluation path must point to `final_dualpath_wide32_seed17.pt`; promotion of the completed T2 top-three winner is historical-only and uses a separately named checkpoint.

## 2026-08-09 — Apply lossless native-resolution memory cleanup

Keep the same FP32 math, T, BPTT, resolution, and model dynamics while removing avoidable RAM use: direct encoding uses a stride-0 temporal view rather than cloning T copies; old batch graph references are released immediately; gradients are cleared with `set_to_none=True` before the next forward; auxiliary heads are skipped in eval/inference; and native-resolution `output_size=None` checkpoints are handled by `evaluate.py`. Do not silently enable activation checkpointing, lower precision, truncated BPTT, or reduced resolution because those trade CPU or change the training calculation.


## 2026-08-09 — Remove obsolete project debris and archive the completed search plan

Keep `src/eventspikeseg/` limited to active runtime/training code plus frozen architecture registries. Delete the obsolete standalone `benchmark_top_three.py`, MEVDT/EventSCNN-only diagnostic scripts, generated `.ruff_cache`, and the PowerShell RTL wrapper that targeted a nonexistent Compose service. Retain the functional local-GHDL `scripts/run_rtl_tests.py`. Move `ddd17-semantic-ablation.md` from `docs/exec-plans/active/` to `completed/` after recording the measured top-three outcome and the feature lineage into the 237,597-parameter canonical model.


## 2026-08-09 — Restrict canonical threshold adaptation to semantic encoder/context

Decision: introduce a canonical-only `encoder_context` threshold-adaptation scope. Historical `encoder` scope semantics remain unchanged. The final model no longer applies triangular threshold adaptation to the full-resolution stem/x1, custom detail branch, or auxiliary head; adaptation begins at x2 after the first spatial downsampling and remains active through x2/x3/context. This matches the intended architecture and removes unnecessary per-pixel temporal state without reducing channels, T, precision, or gradient horizon. Final parameter count becomes 237,597. Stateless convolution plus affine current transforms are vectorized across the T×N batch dimension while PLIF/LIF updates remain sequential, preserving identical temporal dynamics and gradients. The training output contract also uses scalar activity rates instead of retaining complete hidden spike sequences.


## 2026-08-14 — Supersede repeated-static direct encoding with temporal-v2

The 2026-08-05 decision to repeat each six-channel Ev-SegNet sample over internal SNN timesteps is superseded. The timestep sweep and later checkpoint plateau showed that this protocol gives the recurrent neurons repeated current without new sensory evidence and leaves rare classes collapsed.

Decision: remove repeated-static support rather than maintain backward compatibility. Every new sample must contain chronological evidence.

Two supported representations are now first-class:

- `sequential-6ch`: `T` strictly consecutive Ev-SegNet statistical samples from one sequence/interval; target is the final mask.
- `raw-events`: original DDD17 events in a fixed physical interval ending at the label timestamp, divided into `T` ON/OFF count bins.

Training/validation splits are made by complete `sequence_id`, not random frames. The generated dataset-root `ddd17_temporal_manifest.json` is the authoritative source of semantic sequence boundaries and frame order; the runtime no longer generates or infers that chronology from filenames. Raw mode deliberately does **not** store or consume `timestamp_us`/`raw_event` alignment in the manifest: it resolves the canonical `raw/rec<ID>.hdf5` recording and actual APS timestamp in memory from `sequence_id` + `frame_index`. Checkpoints freeze the temporal protocol through a manifest digest plus SHA-256 fingerprint. Checkpoints from the removed repeated-static protocol are deliberately not resumable/evaluable through the current runtime.

Rationale: data chronology is now treated as part of the model contract. Architecture search should resume only after the chronological six-channel and raw-event baselines are measured.

### 2026-08-14 — Raw alignment is runtime data loading, not manifest preprocessing

Decision: remove the separate raw-manifest preparation command and all hard-coded full-dataset count checks. `DDD17RawEventDataset` resolves original DDD17 source frames and real APS timestamps during construction, caches each recording's timestamp table, and never mutates `ddd17_temporal_manifest.json`. The exact terminal-boundary export sample is supported only when its corresponding raw APS frame exists; larger indices fail. Timestamp units are explicit by dtype (float seconds, integer microseconds), and only well-defined raw HDF5 layouts are accepted. This keeps raw alignment in `data.py`, where input extraction belongs, and prevents paper totals from overriding the actual local dataset.

### 2026-08-14 — Supersede HDF5 raw alignment with installed NumPy event bins

The active dataset has no raw HDF5/event-list source. Its event files are dense `200×346×6` Ev-SegNet interval statistics. Exact within-interval `(t,x,y,p)` binning cannot be reconstructed from histogram/mean/std moments.

Decision: make `event-bins` the default input. Each SNN timestep is one strictly consecutive manifest interval; input channels are the two polarity histograms mapped from the stored `[-1,1]` encoding to nonnegative `[0,1]`. Timestamp mean/std channels are not treated as time. `sequential-6ch` remains an ablation. Active training/evaluation has no HDF5 dependency, and the compatibility `train-ddd17-raw` command routes to `event-bins`.

The indexed PNG target remains one class ID per pixel (0–5), while the network emits six spiking class maps. Dataset inspection must report event/label count mismatches; the current installation has 2,887 train event tensors and 10,001 train masks.

## 2026-08-15 — Replace multi-file bins with timestamp-scheduled continuous state

The 2026-08-14 multi-file `event-bins`/`sequential-6ch` decision is superseded.
Each installed NumPy tensor already covers one 50 ms interval and remains one loader
item. Stored order is `hist0,hist1,mean0,std0,mean1,std1`; the loader decodes this
to activity, mean, and standard deviation per polarity.

The input adapter schedules activity at mean and mean±std with weights 0.50/0.25/0.25
over ten 5 ms hardware-clock updates. This conserves activity but is explicitly a
moment approximation because the archive does not contain individual event tuples.
PLIF state persists across consecutive records and resets at sequence/frame gaps.
Training truncates BPTT after each record by detaching, not clearing, recurrent state.
Shuffle, multi-file windows, HDF5/raw readers, user-configured T/stride, and their
compatibility commands are removed. New checkpoints use `ddd17-timestamp-v3`.

The internal clock count is exposed as `--internal-updates` with default 10. It
must divide the physical 50,000 µs interval exactly and is fingerprinted. Continuous
state cleanup includes both project buffers and SpikingJelly's internal PLIF `v`/`w`
references so completed autograd graphs cannot overlap the next record.

## 2026-08-17 — Add DSEC-Semantic as an exact raw-event input contract

DSEC-Semantic is a separate dataset contract, not a conversion into DDD17 moment
maps. The installed eight training sequences contain 8,082 masks and the three test
sequences contain 2,809 masks. Their semantic timestamp files are approximately
50,000 µs apart (20 Hz), despite the historical ESS loader also supporting a 100 ms
subsampled setup.

For every usable label, read `[timestamp-50,000us, timestamp)` directly from the
sequence HDF5 stream using `ms_to_idx`; apply `t_offset`, rectify raw coordinates
with the supplied map, crop to the 640×440 semantic view, and schedule binary ON/OFF
input spikes into `internal_updates` equal physical clocks. Collisions within one
pixel/polarity/clock are binary rather than analog counts. Preserve PLIF state only
across consecutive labels of the same sequence and detach recurrent autograd history
after each mask-aligned record.

The canonical `EventSpikeSeg140k` learned feature topology is unchanged. Its input adapter may
bypass DDD17 moment reconstruction for an already scheduled two-polarity stream, and
its semantic head expands from six to the dataset-required eleven spiking class
populations. DSEC checkpoints use `dsec-raw-events-v1` so they cannot be mixed with
DDD17 checkpoints.

## 2026-08-17 — Add explicit sequential truncated BPTT for DSEC

Dense PyTorch training at ten raw-event clocks retains substantially more temporal
activation memory than model-weight memory. Add `--sequential-chunks`, defaulting to
one so existing training is unchanged. For values above one, divide `[T,N,2,H,W]`
into equal chronological chunks, compute and backpropagate an equally weighted loss
for each chunk immediately, preserve PLIF membrane/adaptation values, and detach
their gradient history before continuing. Optimizer and gradient-accumulation steps
remain record-based rather than chunk-based. This preserves all input clocks and the
forward state trajectory but deliberately truncates gradients at chunk boundaries.

## 2026-08-18 — Preserve surrogate gradients through hard spike gates

A DSEC run remained at zero output spikes and mIoU 0.0081 for six epochs even
though encoder and decoder spike rates were healthy. The loader was not dead: real
records delivered roughly 140k scheduled input spikes and validation showed nonzero
hidden activity. The failure was the neuron output expression `raw * hard_gate`.
When a neuron was below threshold, the hard Boolean gate also multiplied its
surrogate derivative by zero, so a silent class head could never learn toward its
threshold and weight decay pushed it further into silence. Keep the identical binary,
competition-gated forward spike, but use a straight-through expression whose backward
derivative is the surrogate derivative of `raw`. A regression test requires a
subthreshold PLIF neuron to emit zero forward while retaining nonzero input gradient.

## 2026-08-18 — Scale the fixed DSEC validation split with training coverage

The earlier generic greedy sequence split could overshoot a 10% request severely:
it selected `zurich_city_02_a` and the large `zurich_city_05_a`, producing 1,986
validation records (24.6%) and excluding that large route from training. Validation
coverage should instead scale with the amount of data active in each training epoch.

For `train_samples <= 0.5`, exhaustively choose one complete validation sequence;
for a larger fractional training coverage, choose two. Minimize distance from
`total_records × validation_fraction × active_train_fraction`; split seed 17 fixes
ties, and membership remains fixed for the run. On the installed 8,074 usable
records, `train_samples=0.5` selects only the 234-record `zurich_city_02_a`, leaving
7,840 records in the pool and 3,920 in each half-sampled epoch. At `0.7`, validation
uses `zurich_city_01_a` plus `zurich_city_02_a` (914 records).

Fractional training sampling remains independent within every non-validation
sequence, using one deterministic contiguous window per sequence per epoch. Exact
record membership is checkpoint-fingerprinted, so checkpoints from earlier DSEC
splits are not resumed.

## 2026-08-19 — Stabilize DSEC semantic-head optimization

Epoch-12 checkpoint inspection showed a real class collapse: 82.7% of validation
pixels were assigned to building, despite finite model/optimizer tensors and active
hidden spike rates. Three compounding optimizer choices were corrected. Reduce the
DSEC base LR from `1e-3` to `5e-4`; remove the historical `2x` multiplier on PLIF
beta/threshold parameters; and average soft Dice only over classes present in the
current target. Continue reporting the full per-class Dice vector. Median-frequency
class weights remain unchanged for this intervention so their effect can be isolated.

## 2026-08-19 — Keep EventSpikeSeg140k canonical and target digital neuromorphic FPGA inference

Canonical model naming is `EventSpikeSeg140k` for the ~140k deployment baseline and
`EventSpikeSegXL` for the optional 236,445-parameter expanded comparison. This naming
policy does not alter the trained canonical forward topology or parameters.

The canonical six-class model has 140,089 registered parameters, but its executed
inference graph uses 134,637. The inherited `refine2` block (5,056 parameters) is
registered but never called; the 396-parameter auxiliary head is training-only. FPGA
deployment targets the executed inference graph only.

The primary hardware direction is **custom VHDL-2008 on ZCU104**. PyTorch/SpikingJelly
remains the training and floating-point golden-reference stack. Convolution kernels are
shared synaptic weights/connectivity; PLIF feature-map elements are logical neuron states.
FPGA inference represents weights, membrane/current, beta, thresholds, and adaptation
with deterministic fixed-point numbers rather than physical voltages/currents.

The hardware should stay as neuromorphic as practical despite being fully digital:
inter-layer communication is binary spikes/events, active spike addresses trigger shared
weight additions, PLIF state persists in banked memory, and inactivity-aware scheduling
should suppress unnecessary work. The baseline preserves learned per-channel beta with a
quantized coefficient; shift-only/power-of-two leakage is an optimization only if
bit-level behavior and semantic accuracy are revalidated.

Scale is handled with reusable coarse-grained synaptic-accumulator + PLIF processing
elements, not one datapath per logical neuron. The same PEs are time-multiplexed across
spatial tiles/channels/stages while membrane/adaptation state remains in BRAM/URAM/
LUTRAM. Start with exact full/tiled execution, then consider touched-address lists,
active-neuron lists, lazy decay, and clock-enable optimizations.

Nontrivial RTL items are the stem peer-membrane inhibition rule, triangular threshold
adaptation, and the selector's global binary channel enable. The selector `amax(H,W)` can
be implemented efficiently as a per-channel any-spike flag because selector outputs are
binary. Do not silently remove any of these mechanisms; approximations require
revalidation/fine-tuning.

Verification proceeds from SpikingJelly float to a bit-exact integer reference, VHDL RTL
simulation, Vivado synthesis/place-and-route, post-implementation timing simulation, and
representative-event SAIF/VCD power analysis. These Vivado results are estimates. Only
instrumented ZCU104 results may be called measured hardware power/latency.

## 2026-08-20 — Separate shared parameters from per-neuron runtime state for FPGA mapping

Do not use the ~140k parameter count as a proxy for logical PLIF state. Convolution weights
are spatially shared and beta/base threshold are shared per output channel, but every
executed PLIF output element `(layer,c,y,x)` retains its own membrane value. Summing the
37 inference PLIF populations gives `N_mem = (672+Nclass)HW + 480H2W2 + 1280H4W4`.
Corrected for `project2` executing before upsample, six-class DDD17 at 212x282 has
**51,089,456 independent membrane states**.

The threshold-adaptation-enabled scopes contain `352H2W2 + 1216H4W4` logical neurons, or **9,836,800**
at 212x282; each carries adaptation age and adaptation amplitude. These persistent states
are distinct from temporary synaptic-current accumulation and from sparse spike-event FIFOs.

FPGA execution should therefore keep weights/kernel connectivity static/shared while
virtualizing logical neuron state in addressable memory. A small physical PLIF PE array may
be reused across layers and spatial positions, but different logical neuron addresses must
never share one stored membrane value. For exact equivalence, all synaptic contributions for
a layer/timestep are accumulated first, then each PLIF is updated/thresholded once. Hidden
spikes may be processed input-centrically as `(c,y,x)` addresses, so dense sliding-window
convolution is not required. Lazy decay/touched-neuron-only updates remain later
optimizations because they change when decay is evaluated and must be proven equivalent
with reset, peer-membrane inhibition, and triangular threshold adaptation.

## 2026-08-21 — Preserve DSEC event density and supervise complete records

The original DSEC scheduler collapsed every repeated `(clock, polarity, y, x)` event to
one, discarding local event density. It also applied the complete semantic mask to each
temporal chunk independently, so the five-chunk profile imposed the same final target on
five partial 10 ms histories. Replace the external event value with
`min(log2(count + 1), 4)`, retaining unit value for a single event while compressing and
bounding bursts. Hidden inter-layer communication remains binary.

Retain all ten chronological 5 ms clocks but combine their time-normalized logits before
computing weighted CE, present-class Dice, and auxiliary CE. Each 50 ms record now yields
one semantic objective. Numeric membrane/adaptation state continues across consecutive
records and is reset only at epoch, validation, sequence, or frame-gap boundaries.
Chunking remains available for state-carry execution, but its outputs contribute to the
same full-window objective; the compose profiles use one chunk because exact nonlinear
full-window loss must retain the output graphs until backward.

This changes the DSEC temporal protocol to `dsec-log-count-events-full-window-v2`, so old
binary/partial-loss checkpoints are intentionally incompatible. EventSpikeSeg140k remains
the canonical deployment model; EventSpikeSegXL is an explicitly named comparison run.

## 2026-08-21 — Make DSEC deployment asynchronous and training timestamp-aware (superseded 2026-08-22)

The bounded-log/full-window decision above still described a clocked input model and is
superseded for DSEC. The final FPGA must accept sequential camera events directly; it must
