not store 5 ms input bins or run a global neural timestep. Each touched neuron stores a
last-update timestamp, applies `beta ** (delta_us / 5000)`, receives the shared synaptic
weight, thresholds/resets, and immediately enqueues generated binary spikes. State carries
across semantic labels and resets only at sequence/frame gaps.

PyTorch/SpikingJelly remains the fast training backend. It accumulates chronological
source events into `internal_updates` fixed physical-time divisions, preserves linear
event multiplicity without log/cap, and supplies the fixed elapsed duration to the same
physical-time neuron equation. Event density never changes the number of training updates.
These divisions are a training approximation, not an FPGA input representation.

All packets in the 50 ms label interval contribute to one final semantic objective. For
memory-bounded XL training, first form the complete-record prediction without retaining
activation graphs, then restore initial neuron state and replay chronological chunks using
the one objective's output gradients. Keep the replay snapshot in host RAM to avoid a
second full persistent-state copy on the GPU. This is truncated state-gradient history,
but it is not multiple partial-history target losses. Logits/rates are normalized by
elapsed physical time so packet count does not change their scale.

The global spatial selector gate is incompatible with immediate event propagation. Replace
its `amax(H,W)` channel enable with a local retinotopic 1x1-PLIF binary gate. The new DSEC
protocol is `dsec-fixed-time-counts-async-fpga-v4`; older checkpoints are rejected.

## 2026-08-21 — Use EventSpikeSegXL as the detailed DSEC/FPGA implementation target

Promote `EventSpikeSegXL` from an undocumented capacity comparison to the active detailed
implementation target for the 11-class DSEC configuration at `241x351`. Retain
`EventSpikeSeg140k` as the compact reference/baseline; this decision does not rename either
Python model or claim that XL has already achieved better measured accuracy.

The instantiated XL/DSEC module has 237,115 registered parameters. Its custom forward omits
the inherited 5,056-parameter `refine2`; training additionally executes 726 parameters in
the mid auxiliary head and 4,566 in the deep auxiliary head. The deployed inference graph
therefore contains 226,767 parameters and 50 PLIF populations. FPGA export must omit the
dead block and both training-only heads.

For `H=241`, `W=351`, `H2=121`, `W2=176`, `H4=61`, and `W4=88`, XL inference owns
`683HW + 768H2W2 + 2144H4W4 = 85,639,973` membrane states. Its 26 threshold-adaptation-enabled
populations contain `416H2W2 + 2080H4W4 = 20,024,576` neurons, each with adaptation age
and amplitude. Direct asynchronous DSEC execution also requires one last-update timestamp
per membrane state. An illustrative exact store using 16-bit membrane, 16-bit age and
amplitude, and 32-bit timestamps is about 566.42 MiB before FIFOs, caches, accumulators,
and output counters. Therefore the XL baseline uses an external-DDR state store with an
on-chip banked tile/cache; an all-on-chip state claim requires a later quantified
compression/quantization result.

The parallel context and local Q/K/V spike-mask attention are part of the executed graph.
They require timestamp-tagged local join/rendezvous buffers so residual branches and Q/K/V
paths are combined deterministically without introducing a global spatial-frame barrier.

## 2026-08-22 — Freeze 140k and use permanent layer actors on U55C

This decision supersedes the 2026-08-21 XL target/platform mapping. The only FPGA target
is the two-input, 11-class `EventSpikeSeg140k`: 140,589 registered parameters, 134,807
executed inference parameters, 127,456 executed convolution weights, and 37 executed
PLIF populations. FPGA export hard-rejects XL and excludes inherited dead `refine2`, the
training-only auxiliary head, and surrogate gradients.

Instantiate all 37 executed populations as permanent layer actors. Each actor owns its
convolution address generator, synaptic accumulator lanes, PLIF/inhibition/adaptation
logic, output FIFO, and initialized local weight ROM. Do not share compute across layers,
reconfigure actors, or use partial reconfiguration. An actor may sequence its own lanes
over that actor's logical destinations. Clock/reset, timestamp input dispatch, HBM state
fabric, and final class readout remain permitted shared infrastructure.

Choose AMD Alveo U55C as the primary board and U280 as fallback. All executed int8
weights fit comfortably in local BRAM/LUTROM, but the corrected 140k graph has 72,843,333 logical
membranes and 14,023,680 adaptation-enabled neurons at 241x351. The baseline 16-bit
membrane, 32-bit timestamp, and two 16-bit adaptation fields require about 470.31 MiB
before queues/counters. Therefore exact persistent state uses in-package HBM2 behind
actor-local BRAM/URAM caches. This is not off-board DDR and does not change event-driven
execution.

Iteration 0 freezes the format/graph/export boundary before new integrated RTL: emit a
37-actor manifest, affine-folded int8 weight ROMs, quantized neuron/mechanism parameters,
and deterministic fixed-point rules; validate integer PLIF and sparse-state primitives;
then generate actor packages and layer-by-layer golden traces. Existing VHDL files remain
isolated historical primitives until they are proven against this new contract.

## 2026-08-22 — Replace the accelerator card with no-DDR Artix-7

This supersedes the U55C/HBM platform portion of the preceding decision, while retaining
the frozen 140k graph and 37 permanent actors. Use the conventional Digilent Nexys Video
with XC7A200T-1SBG484C. Do not use its 512 MiB DDR3 or any other external memory.

The device provides 13,140 Kibit = 1.604 MiB BRAM and at most 2,888 Kibit = 0.3525 MiB
distributed RAM. The dense 140k runtime state is 481.899 MiB, so BRAM alone is short by
468.705 MiB/293.209x. Even sacrificing all distributed RAM gives only 1.9565 MiB and
would leave insufficient LUT logic. Static int8 convolution weights are only 0.122 MiB
and do fit; persistent spatial neuron state is the incompatibility.

The only semantics-preserving route that might remain on this device is a lossless
zero-default sparse live-state table with no overflow eviction or spill. Full DSEC traces
must prove a worst-case occupancy bound including tags, values, queues, weights, and logic
margin. Until it passes, an all-on-chip full-resolution implementation is explicitly
classified as infeasible rather than assumed to fit.

## 2026-08-22 — Preserve named neuron runtime state in training checkpoints (superseded 2026-08-23)

Keep recurrent tensors nonpersistent in the ordinary PyTorch `state_dict`, because their
data-dependent shapes make strict weight loading fragile. Instead, only the best-model
checkpoint stores a separate `training_runtime_state` mapping keyed by executed layer name;
periodic and `last.pt` recovery checkpoints remain normal-sized. The canonical checkpoint
inherits the snapshot when the best checkpoint is copied to it. The snapshot contains the
membrane, adaptation, threshold-timer/adaptation, and physical-time state captured at the
end of the training epoch before validation resets or overwrites it. The format and scope
fields identify this as `layer-named-hardware-lif-v1` forensic state. Normal resume remains
epoch-boundary weight/optimizer restoration; replaying saved runtime state must be explicit
so it cannot leak one sequence into another.

This one-off forensic snapshot was removed on 2026-08-23. Best, periodic, last, and
canonical checkpoints now contain ordinary trainable model/optimizer/protocol state only;
they do not serialize data-dependent membrane, adaptation, or elapsed-time tensors.

## 2026-08-28 — Stabilize class learning with blended pixel and macro CE

Replace the final suite's pure per-record macro CE with 0.5 pixel-mean CE plus
0.5 target-present-class macro CE. Retain rare-class pressure without allowing a
very small person or traffic-sign region to determine the full record's CE gradient.
Increase present-class Dice from 0.2 to 0.3, use pixel-mean CE for the training-only
semantic auxiliary heads at weight 0.1, and reduce weighted edge-head BCE from 0.1
to 0.05. Keep focal and static class weights disabled, retain activity regularisation,
and continue evaluating exactly one objective after each complete 50 ms record.


## 2026-08-28 — Add state-safe raw-event augmentation

Enable training-only DSEC horizontal flip at probability 0.5, integer translation
within plus or minus six output pixels, and zero-to-five-percent event dropout. Apply
one spatial transform to the raw rectified event coordinates and aligned target mask;
all ten 5 ms updates in the 50 ms record share it. Because membrane state persists
across records, keep the spatial transform fixed for each recording sequence for the
complete run. Event dropout may vary deterministically by epoch and record without
moving surviving events. Fill translated mask borders with ignore index 255, preserve
event timestamps, polarities, multiplicities, and order, and leave validation untouched.

Move DSEC HDF5 reading, rectification, augmentation, mask loading, and temporal binning
off the main training process into two CPU DataLoader workers. Prefetch only one complete
record per worker into pinned host RAM and use non-blocking CUDA transfer. This overlaps
CPU preparation with GPU training without increasing the model's active-record VRAM
requirement or changing chronological delivery. Set the DSEC Docker shared-memory ceiling
to 256 MiB because the 64 MiB default cannot hold two native-resolution records plus
PyTorch multiprocessing queue overhead; this ceiling is consumed on demand and is not
reserved VRAM or an immediate host-RAM allocation.


## 2026-08-23 — Replace static class weights with macro per-class loss in every suite

DSEC person pixels occupy about 0.078% of the installed training masks and traffic-sign
pixels about 0.325%. A capped static weight cannot make their contribution comparable to
large road/building regions. For every suite, compute mean negative log-probability
separately for each target-present class and average those class means equally. Apply the
same macro rule to focal loss where a historical profile enables focal, and to semantic
auxiliary heads. Classes absent from a record have no positive
term, but their logits remain competitors in the softmax denominator. Retain 0.2 present-
class Dice, 0.1 auxiliary CE, 0.1 boundary supervision, and activity regularisation; keep
focal disabled in the final profile. Existing historical measurements remain records of
their original objectives; new comparison runs use the unified macro balancing rule.


## 2026-08-22 — Use 50 ms record-level, T=10 fixed-step FPGA inference

This supersedes the 2026-08-21 decision that made DSEC deployment direct asynchronous AER execution. Preserve the trained timing contract instead of translating the checkpoint to a new event-time neuron model.

DSEC still starts from exact raw `(t,x,y,p)` events, but both software training and baseline FPGA inference assign every event to one of ten chronological 5 ms slices inside the 50 ms semantic interval. Event multiplicity is preserved. The network therefore executes ten fixed PLIF updates per record and emits one segmentation result after temporal class-spike counting over the complete 50 ms interval.

The FPGA scheduling order is intentionally **layer-major over the complete T=10 record**. For layer `L`, process slices `t0..t9` sequentially using that layer's own persistent membrane/adaptation state, buffer its ten output spike slices, then let layer `L+1` consume that sequence. This is equivalent to the trained multi-step feed-forward graph because recurrent state is local to each PLIF population.

This scheduling choice is also the baseline state-memory strategy. Load a layer/state tile once, keep it resident while executing all ten 5 ms updates, and write its final state back after `t9`. Sparse event-address convolution remains enabled within each slice, so zero spike locations do not trigger synaptic additions. The baseline explicitly advances every required state in known 5 ms steps and therefore does not require a 32-bit `last_update_us` field per membrane. Timestamp/lazy-decay storage becomes an optional later optimization only if the integer reference proves exact equivalence.

The runtime is **not fully asynchronous**. The real-time throughput target is one complete 50 ms record every 50 ms (20 Hz); record acquisition and compute may be double-buffered. The ten internal 5 ms steps may not be collapsed into a single 50 ms PLIF update.

## 2026-08-23 — Native-resolution learned downsampling and continuous class readout

The 241x351 v6 graph plateaued below the required accuracy and discarded spatial evidence
before the first learned operation. Supersede it with the v7 `EventSpikeSeg140k` graph.
DSEC raw events and labels remain at the canonical 440x640 semantic geometry. Do not add
raw max pooling: the semantic branch begins with a learned standard 5x5 stride-2 PLIF
stem, while a narrow 8-channel native PLIF path preserves thin objects and boundaries.

Use standard spatial/channel convolution for the native detail block, first 32-channel
semantic block, and 32->64 transition. Retain depthwise/pointwise processing deeper in
the network. This improves early cross-channel spatial modelling while keeping the model
near the original weight budget and reducing persistent state relative to naively running
the old full-resolution trunk at 440x640.

Remove the local 96-channel selector, 32-channel native decoder refinement, and spiking
class head. Fuse native semantic32 + detail8 through DW40/PW16 PLIF, then accumulate the
16->11 synaptic current continuously over all ten updates. The loss is still evaluated
once per complete 50 ms record. The continuous readout is stateless between records and
maps to conditional signed weight additions plus integer accumulators on FPGA.

Add a training-only native boundary head with coefficient 0.1 while retaining weighted
CE, 0.2 present-class Dice, and the 0.1 semantic auxiliary CE. Change the DSEC compose
profile to native 440x640 and ten replay chunks to bound the activation graph to one
5 ms update. Keep `artifacts/checkpointsV3/dsec` unchanged, but old v6 checkpoints are
architecturally incompatible and this run must start from scratch.

The new 11-class graph has 142,277 registered parameters, 141,535 executed inference
parameters, 135,624 executed synaptic weights, 27 PLIF populations, 43,366,400 logical
membranes, and 10,982,400 adaptation-enabled states. Dense 16-bit state is 124.61 MiB
before queues/alignment, so the no-DDR sparse-state feasibility gate remains in force.

## 2026-08-23 — Adopt v7 native-resolution state-first graph and migrate RTL to fixed-step Artix-7 baseline

Codex's v7 canonical `EventSpikeSeg140k` is now the sole hardware graph: native 440x640 DSEC input, 27 deployed PLIF populations, 135,624 executed synaptic weights, 43,366,400 membrane states, and a stateless 16->11 temporal class-current readout. The wide 32-channel semantic trunk begins at half resolution after a learned 5x5 stride-2 PLIF stem; the native detail path is only 8 channels. The old 96-channel selector, native decoder refinement, and output class PLIF population are removed.

This revision is accepted as the new state-first architecture because it increases static synaptic weights by about 6.4% relative to v6 while reducing PLIF populations by 27.0%, logical membrane state by about 40.5%, and dense persistent state from about 192.44 MiB to 124.61 MiB even though v7 runs at native 440x640. Hardware architecture search must continue to treat writable neuron state as more expensive than static weight ROM.

## 2026-08-24 — Preserve spike evidence through decoder and native fusion (v8)

The v7 quarter skip used binary OR before a DW->PLIF->PW decoder block, and the native
semantic/detail concatenation also crossed a depthwise PLIF before any channel mixing.
Those intermediate thresholds could discard weak small-object evidence distributed across
encoder, decoder, semantic, and detail channels. Replace quarter OR plus DW/PW refinement
with channel concatenation and one standard 1x1 128->64 PLIF fusion. At the half skip,
retain coincident encoder/decoder events as multiplicity two rather than OR-deduplicating
them. Replace native DW40->PW16 with one standard 3x3 40->16 PLIF so spatial and channel
evidence accumulates jointly before thresholding.

The 11-class graph now has 150,245 registered parameters, 149,503 executed inference
parameters, 143,904 executed synaptic weights, 25 PLIF populations, and 30,976,000 logical
membranes. Threshold-adaptation scope remains 10,982,400 neurons. Compared with v7 this
adds 8,280 static synaptic weights but removes 12,390,400 persistent membrane values and
two PLIF actors. Dense exact membrane/adaptation/24-bit-tag storage falls from 248.68 MiB
to 189.60 MiB. This is accepted as the accuracy/state trade because static ROM growth is
small while fusion becomes less lossy and writable state falls by 28.6%.

The FPGA family is explicitly **AMD/Xilinx Artix-7**, with `XC7A200T-1SBG484C` on Digilent Nexys Video as the reference implementation board. The board DDR3 remains forbidden by the active no-external-memory experiment.

The baseline runtime is fixed-time and identical to training: one 50 ms record contains ten ordered 5 ms steps, executed layer-major. Remove the superseded per-neuron `last_timestamp_us` state and `beta**(delta/5ms)` runtime coefficient path. `plif_update` consumes learned beta directly once per 5 ms step, `sparse_state_bank` stores no timestamp, and `fixed_record_controller` sequences 27 PLIF layers x 10 steps. The final readout accumulates signed current and performs first-index-tie argmax; do not recreate output PLIF/class-spike counting.


## 2026-08-23 — Stream live DSEC in 5 ms bins and preserve touched-neuron lazy state

This supersedes the same-day 50 ms layer-major FPGA scheduling decision while leaving training unchanged. Live camera events are accumulated into consecutive 5 ms ON/OFF bins. One sealed bin must traverse the full deployed graph (27 PLIF actors plus the stateless class-current update) before the bin is retired. Input uses ping-pong buffers so bin `k+1` is captured while bin `k` computes.

The current DSEC training path enables touched-neuron lazy updates. Runtime must therefore preserve that behavior: each live sparse neuron state stores a compact 24-bit `last_bin_tag`; when touched at bin `k`, compute `delta_bins = k - last_bin_tag (mod 2^24)` and apply the learned beta once per elapsed bin before adding current. The first RTL implementation uses an iterative beta multiply so long-idle neurons consume additional cycles; a beta-power LUT/accelerator is allowed later only with bit-exact validation. No 32-bit microsecond timestamp is used.

## 2026-08-24 — Save fixed qualitative DSEC validation previews every epoch

Choose three validation-record indices once from the run seed and reuse those records for
every epoch. Capture their outputs inside the existing chronological validation pass so
recurrent state stays correct and validation does not require extra forward executions.
Save `event | ground truth | prediction` PNG panels under `<checkpoint-dir>/images`.
The event panel collapses all ten 5 ms divisions into the complete 50 ms count map:
polarity 0 is red (negative), polarity 1 is blue (positive), and a shared logarithmic
count scale controls intensity so both low- and high-count pixels remain visible.

The signed 16->11 class-current readout accumulates over a configurable prediction window. Start with ten bins, therefore one prediction every 50 ms / 20 Hz, because that matches training. PLIF/adaptation state continues across prediction boundaries and resets only at sequence/frame gaps.

Define real-time utilization `U = C_bin / (f_clk * 5 ms)` and throughput headroom `H = 1/U`. Sustained operation requires `U <= 1` (`H >= 1`). The starting engineering target is `U <= 0.8` (`H >= 1.25`), leaving 20% timing margin. At 100/150/200 MHz the hard budgets are 500k/750k/1M cycles per bin and the 80% target budgets are 400k/600k/800k cycles. These values must come from measured controller cycle counts after synthesis/RTL integration, not from parameter count.

## 2026-08-25 — Replace the global FPGA scheduler with permanent autonomous ASIC actors

Keep all 25 PLIF populations resident and prohibit runtime layer reconfiguration. A standard
3x3 convolution plane uses nine parallel presynaptic tap projection cells. Each tap applies
the YAML scatter address equation and emits a signed weight contribution; taps do not own
membranes. All contributions for `(layer, output-channel, y, x)` accumulate in one canonical
actor-local current slot and update one actor-local membrane entry.

Preserve the trained 5 ms time semantics with ordered completion tokens. A receiving actor
accepts sparse contributions until its upstream token arrives, commits every touched PLIF
exactly once, emits its spikes, and then forwards completion. This is logically asynchronous
dataflow without a central actor scheduler. The initial VHDL is still a clocked ready/valid
reference; no clockless/QDI claim is made.

Keep VHDL-2008 and replace Vivado for ASIC PPA with GHDL -> Yosys -> OpenROAD. Use ASAP7
only for reproducible predictive 7 nm logic PPA and SKY130RAM only for a manufacturable
tile experiment. Neither is a full-chip tapeout decision. The native graph has 30,976,000
membranes and 189.60 MiB of dense exact state under the current tag/adaptation contract, so
credible full-chip area/power requires characterized high-density SRAM macros and an
accessible 22 nm-or-better foundry PDK. Flip-flop-expanded behavioral arrays are forbidden
as a claimed full-model PPA result.

## 2026-08-25 — Use native SystemVerilog and sequential raw-event ASIC ingress

This supersedes the VHDL/GHDL portion and the ping-pong-bin ingress portion of the preceding
ASIC decision. Implement the ASIC directly in synthesizable SystemVerilog and use
Icarus/cocotb, Yosys, and OpenROAD. Preserve the FPGA attempt in the non-normative
`legacy_fpga_history` section of `project_spec.yaml`, the chronological decision/progress
logs, and Git history.

The ASIC camera interface accepts rectified raw events sequentially in timestamp order.
Each event immediately triggers only its connected static-weight additions; it does not
load a dense 5 ms tensor and does not fetch or update unrelated neuron state. A completion
token is inserted at each trained 5 ms boundary. That token causes each actor to commit
only its touched PLIF destinations once, emit spikes, and forward completion.

Do not threshold or reset a PLIF after every individual signed contribution. Training
performs one PLIF update per 5 ms division after convolutional accumulation, and signed
kernel contributions can cancel. Immediate per-event firing would therefore be a different
event-time neuron model and would require explicit event-driven retraining and accuracy
validation. The current ASIC is sparse and event-sequential in its synaptic work while
retaining the trained 5 ms PLIF commit semantics.

## 2026-08-25 — Treat the 25 ASIC actors as the exact v8 DAG, never as a linear pipeline

`EventSpikeSeg140k` deploys 25 PLIF populations, but their enumeration is only a stable
topological/export order. Runtime interconnect must reproduce the actual model DAG. Input
events fork into `detail_stem` and `stem`; residual blocks binary-OR their bypass and main
spikes; `x1` and `x2` fan out into deeper encoder and later decoder routes; `project3` and
`project2` use 2x nearest-event fanout; decoder concatenations use fixed channel remapping;
`project2 + x1` deliberately preserves coincident events as multiplicity two; semantic and
detail routes converge before `final_fuse.standard`.

Completion tokens follow the same directed routes. A fanout duplicates the tagged token to
every required branch. A multi-input residual/skip/concat node may emit one downstream
completion only after all required upstream tokens for the same 5 ms bin have arrived. This
rendezvous rule is required for correctness: a fast branch must not close a downstream PLIF
epoch before a slower skip/detail branch has contributed.

Use compile-time dedicated routing rather than a programmable NoC unless later evidence
shows programmability is worth its area/energy cost. Actor-local membrane/adaptation SRAM
remains unchanged by this decision; only spikes and completion tokens traverse the graph.
## 2026-09-01 — Replace single-lane actor service with destination-banked lanes

Keep the exact 25 permanent PLIF-population DAG and the 5 ms completion-token semantics of the
trained checkpoint. Do not reinterpret one actor as one physical neuron: the model owns
30,976,000 logical `(actor,channel,y,x)` PLIF states, so each actor remains an addressable local
population. Replace the current one-port/one-update-lane actor organization with deterministic
destination banking. Each lane owns disjoint state/current/queue SRAM rows, and contributions are
routed by a compile-time address-to-bank mapping. Reconstruct global spike addresses at lane egress
and merge completion only after every lane has retired the same bin.

Parallel arithmetic without independent SRAM/queue ports does not count as lane scaling. Preserve
signed saturating current accumulation, per-destination ordering, one PLIF commit per touched
destination per bin, lazy last-bin tags, and exact spike backpressure. First implement and prove the
ordinary non-retinotopic actor bank wrapper used by the measured `final_fuse.standard` bottleneck;
then add multi-output convolution scatter and mechanism-enabled spatial/channel-group banking.
Keep the existing single-lane RTL as the bit-exact reference and service-bound baseline.

The lane count is workload- and clock-profiled rather than fixed at 570. The current epoch-40
5 ms sample establishes only an ideal lower bound; final sizing must include bank conflicts,
scatter bandwidth, routing, FIFO occupancy, the 0.8 utilization target, and full-sequence DSEC
activity. A fully event-continuous PLIF that fires after each raw event is a different temporal
model and requires retraining or accuracy revalidation; it is not silently substituted here.

Mechanism-enabled actors may partition state only by spatial location. All channels at one
`(y,x)` location must stay in the same lane because peer inhibition and triangular threshold
adaptation are computed as one retinotopic group. Ordinary actors may partition by both output
channel and spatial address. In both cases, the scatter fabric must preserve contribution order
within a destination bank; cross-bank work may proceed concurrently.

Place one ordered elastic FIFO on every generated DAG edge. Spike packets and completion tokens
share the same queue so a token cannot overtake contributions from its bin. A full FIFO applies
ready/valid backpressure rather than dropping data; depth affects elasticity and timing, not model
semantics. Keep depth compile-time configurable and measure native occupancy before replacing the
default depth of two with per-edge values.

Treat busiest-bank service interval and same-bin response latency as separate acceptance gates.
Throughput uses the maximum complete bank workload; response latency has an additional unavoidable
floor from the longest topological chain of post-token PLIF commits. A lane manifest is accepted
only after exact native reprofiling passes both the hard and 80% floors. Heuristic lane planning may
choose a candidate, but its projection is never reported as measured latency.

Apply a third independent gate to every ordered graph edge: packet count divided by physical link
width cannot exceed the bin cycle budget. State-bank parallelism does not compensate for a scalar
link. Widen a proven hot path or raise the physically closed full-chip clock; do not claim either
from an unconstrained analytical rate. FIFO depth cannot repair a sustained serialization-rate
failure.

For inhibition/adaptation actors, distinguish accumulation ownership from commit ownership.
Synaptic currents and persistent per-channel PLIF state may use independent channel-by-spatial SRAM
ports. A spatial coordinator owns the completion-time transaction: it reads every peer channel for
one `(y,x)`, computes the retinotopic mechanism once, and commits peer results atomically. The old
spatial-only contribution port is rejected because it serializes all output channels before the
mechanism is even evaluated. For ordinary actors, replace the one-event pending bitmap with
decoupled per-bank projection queues or multiple ordered contexts so one busy bank cannot stall all
independent destinations.

Use a distinct small ordered contribution FIFO per destination bank between projection generation
and state SRAM. The FIFO stores the complete destination plus signed weight, preserves saturating
addition order within that bank, and must drain before the actor accepts the bin completion token.
Its depth is a measured elasticity parameter; it is not a substitute for sufficient sustained bank
bandwidth.

The final direct-state ASIC uses register/latch-resident current, membrane, and enabled adaptation
state for every logical PLIF neuron; it does not use an addressed state SRAM or touched-address
queue. Every 5 ms completion applies one beta step to all resident neurons in parallel, which is
equivalent to the prior lazy elapsed-bin update without storing a last-bin tag. Convolution weights
remain compile-time constants at their owning synapses. This deliberately exchanges dense local
state area for elimination of memory-fetch latency; physical area and clock/power must be measured
after the complete resident graph is synthesized and placed.

The inter-actor representation for this implementation is a binary spike vector per PLIF
population and bin. Static convolutional synapses are compile-time wires/constants that reduce
the asserted presynaptic bits into a signed current delta for every destination in parallel.
More than one accepted vector transaction in a bin adds the delta again, preserving multiplicity
at joins or ingress. A held result vector is also the actor's completion transaction, including
the 24-bit bin tag. This removes the scalar per-channel contribution bottleneck as well as the
addressed state fetch, at the explicit cost of wide routing and replicated adder logic.

Wide fanout must not silently allocate a second full feature-map register bank. The resident
fork holds the producing actor's ready/valid vector stable and records only whether each branch
has accepted it. Residual branches rendezvous and OR complete binary vectors; concatenation
remaps fixed channel slices; the half-resolution decoder sum forwards both vector pulses before
completion so coincident activity causes two downstream additions. Raw ingress remains scalar
and is decoded into a one-hot pulse independently at each first-layer branch.
## 2026-09-07 — Fuse resident current/membrane storage and compact clocked adaptation

Replace the active direct resident ASIC's separate int24 current and int16 membrane arrays
with one signed int32 Q15.16 combined accumulator/state element per logical PLIF. After each fixed
5 ms completion, perform clamp, threshold/reset, peer inhibition, and adaptation atomically,
then pre-apply one learned-beta step into the same element for the next bin. Events add signed
contributions to that prepared element. This preserves the discrete-time equation when int32
does not saturate and removes a permanent int16 array; overflow instrumentation and bit-exact
regression remain mandatory.

Store weights and membrane/threshold values as signed Q7.16 in 24 bits, the combined accumulator
as signed Q15.16 in 32 bits, and beta as unsigned UQ1.23 in 24 bits. Store triangular adaptation
age as an unsigned 6-bit integer count of 5 ms bins with value 63 reserved for inactive,
amplitude and strength as unsigned Q3.16 in 19 bits, and duration as unsigned Q5.16 in 21 bits.
Age is deliberately integer rather
than fixed point because it advances only by whole clocked bins. The epoch-80 140k checkpoint's
15 enabled populations learn durations 4.82625..5.22349 bins and strengths
0.185593..0.219574; XL training currently has only an early checkpoint, not a completed model.
The selected widths cover the configured
duration maximum of 16 bins and the worst configured amplitude bound of membrane-energy clip
1.5 times strength maximum 4.0, rather than depending only on that checkpoint.

The 140k logical resident-state inventory falls from 189.60 to 150.89 MiB-equivalent. An XL
mapping under the same contract calculates to 347.66 MiB instead of 436.00 MiB.

## 2026-09-07 — Promote EventSpikeSegXL to a prepared ASIC target

Supersede the earlier software-only/export-rejection decision. The exporter now recognizes the
exact eleven-class XL checkpoint contract: 44 inference PLIF actors, 959,824 signed Q7.16
convolution weights, four Q7.16 spike-attention thresholds, a 24-to-11 temporal current readout,
78,707,200 combined accumulator states, and 15,910,400 compact adaptation states. Training-only
mid/deep auxiliary heads remain excluded.

The static DAG includes both projected downsample bridges, two capacity residual blocks, the
three-way dilated context, asymmetric detail refinement, decoder refinements, and a fully-spiking
four-head attention primitive. Per location/head, the primitive popcounts eight Q/K spike
coincidences, compares the learned fixed threshold, gates V spikes, then feeds the existing
spiking output projection and binary residual join. It never creates softmax or a dense NxN map.

`EventSpikeSegXL_seed17_epoch001.pt` is authorized only for architecture, shapes, constants-flow,
and RTL-generation bring-up while training is active. It is not the final accuracy checkpoint.
Regenerate the ignored XL export from the selected final/QAT checkpoint before equivalence or PPA.
Preparation may run lightweight export/generation/tests, but Yosys/OpenROAD execution requires
explicit user approval because training and synthesis compete for host memory.

## 2026-09-15 — Use exact inventory plus representative physical characterization

Estimate full-model feasibility by routing and activity-characterizing one physical
instance for each block class, then scaling those measurements with
the exact native physical-instance inventory. Include the
readout and report area, leakage, dynamic energy, average power, and efficiency with
measured coverage and a sensitivity band. Preserve the exact graph and count its
98 edges, fanouts, joins, completion tokens, and macro receptive halos; do not
run a whole-model simulation or invent wirelength before top-level placement.

Use 1x1 physical leaves for every actor. Exact 8x8 and 4x4 actor trials exceeded
the 20 GiB guarded implementation limit during mapping; the 2x2 trial reached
detailed routing but was OOM-killed at a 20,980,348 KiB peak. This changes
partitioning, not model semantics: the logical 8x8 hierarchy, all 44 actors, all
78,707,200 PLIF identities, and the native 440x640 result remain exact.

Call the scaled result a representative-block hierarchical full-model estimate.
Reserve full-chip routed PPA for the completed catalog views plus top-level

# 2026-09-24 — switched-capacitor path remains active; resolve small-weight floor

The active analog reference is the ordinary best-validation float32 checkpoint; quantized/QAT values are not required for an analog design. The switched-capacitor MOS virtual-summing-node cell remains the active exploration path, but the physical state interface is failing. Corrected baseline-subtracted packet curves are monotonic in both signs, while the full-float software sensitivity still leaves 26.1–54.6% of final-fusion weights below the measured floor. Direct TG initialization produces 0.259–0.326V state error; a held-output variant produces 0.050–0.117V error, and a coarse 3×3 plus fine 4×2 bias sweep has no acceptable combination of state accuracy and hold drift: the best state match drifts 49mV and the least-drift point misses state by 62mV. Those packet curves and output agreements do not qualify the hardware. Next debug the OTA output equilibrium and state isolation, then return to offset correction and full-float mapping.

## 2026-09-24 — matched reference cancels nominal reconnect common mode

Keep the full-float trained checkpoint as the analog reference; QAT and quantized exports do not set analog precision. The late-switched VCM-referenced readout works across event orders in TT, but its fixed gain varies across process corners: gain 1.0 is accurate in TT/FF and misses SS by 17.37µV; gain 1.35 corrects SS but over-gains TT by 21.49µV. The OTA input-size sweep did not find a shared solution. Its 1GΩ input/feedback network is also idealized and lacks physical resistor geometry. Continue analog work by evaluating a trim-calibrated gain and a switched-capacitor readout with physical-sized parts; mismatch and layout qualification follow only after that blocker is resolved.

## 2026-09-25 — readout direction: active differential sensing

The passive MIM sampler is not the preferred readout: reducing its size leaves estimated kT/C above the event signal, while enlarging it approaches the signal scale only by adding membrane loading; nominal feedthrough also requires an unimplemented baseline correction. A direct OTA differential input has sufficient nominal small-signal sensitivity for a 64µV differential, but its present open-loop gain saturates by roughly 250µV. Keep the passive sampler only as a comparator/control. Noise, PVT, mismatch and physical implementation remain open.

An idealized software first-touch clamp largely removes the two-frame mean-IoU collapse seen when independent reset noise is imposed on every state at record initialization. It does not establish hardware feasibility or model fidelity across the validation set. A physical SKY13010µm MIM/TG/MOS timing screen shows that the clamp must open before the injector turns on:0.2–10ns lead gives the reference≈16.68µV retained contribution, simultaneous opening gives≈157µV, and1ns late gives≈3.22mV. Use this as a provisional local two-phase ordering requirement. Next qualify switch thermal noise, process/temperature/mismatch timing, untouched-state retention, first-touch detection and full-population event throughput. Reject the per-state OTA/output-MIM baseline sampler; keep layout and architecture acceptance gated.

Selected SS/FF MOS corners at0/85°C leave the release-order condition intact but vary the same fixed-gate event contribution37.3× (3.77–140.59µV). Ideal per-corner gate adjustments can match this one TT step to within about2%, but no on-chip trim, per-weight curve or mismatch evidence exists. The next analog circuit choice must include a stable or calibrated charge-per-event source; do not treat nominal fixed-gate mapping as a qualified weight encoding.

First-touch also does not remove the resident-state area gate. A3µm pair reduces bare all-state MIM area to557.568mm², but its639.12µV independent-noise proxy lowers selected two-frame mean IoU to0.14377/0.15201. Keep denser state/reference methods in exploration rather than replicating the current MIM pair. No layout-rule legality or dataset-wide accuracy has been established for3µm.

Do not select a sparse exact-zero analog state pool on the current evidence. Two full-float validation records have75–76% nonzero membranes after the first update and82% after the tenth across all25 actors, leaving little nominal capacity saving before address, switch and adaptation overhead. This is a bounded software observation; revisit only with broader evidence or a specific physical compression mechanism.

A one-local-cap plus ideal shared-reference proxy halves bare MIM area and improves first-touch replay over an independent pair, but a10µm capacitor still costs3,097.6mm² across the full inventory, while5µm loses mean IoU on both sampled frames. No physical reference distribution/noise or single-ended comparator has passed. Keep it as a bound, not a chosen topology.

Do not assume one scalar PVT gain trim fixes the fixed-width MOS weight encoding. A solver-controlled physical two-point comparison changes the0.4/0.6 gate-charge ratio by1.62× across selected TT/SS/FF temperatures. Small-weight measurements from the looser multi-case sweep are not accepted as calibration data because they vary with solver settings. Pursue a reproducible nonlinear mapping or a more stable signed charge source before choosing a weight DAC and layout.

Keep a fixed pulse width **per stage** as a provisional range option: extending the whole stage's pulse from10.5ns to at most≈444ns could span its largest sampled float weight at the slow corner, while sampled p99 pulse service remains≤15.74µs. This is distinct from the rejected long weight-specific duration encoding. The positive physical MOS event is nearly linear in width at zero state through450ns, but changes5.35% over prepared−0.9…+0.9V differential and its smallest gate-fraction charge is far below noise qualification. Do not select the injector or layout from this range-only evidence; nonlinear PVT calibration, state dependence, low-weight noise, signed polarity and full-fanin task replay remain required.

Nominal positive and negative 10-event sequences retain the selected source as a circuit research candidate, not as a qualified float synapse. At40ns, same-sign error is0.0731% but alternating-sign cancellation leaves2.135µV residual, material next to the sampled final-fusion threshold scale; at450ns the alternating residual is0.2657mV. Use real signed weight mixtures and model replay before deciding whether calibration or a different charge topology is needed. Matched PDK symmetry does not establish mismatch tolerance.

Actual epoch58 weighted fan-in keeps the fixed stage-width amplitude injector as the provisional analog event source. At the370-event SS/0°C final-fusion site, a directly measured40.253ns charge curve with log-charge interpolation cuts deterministic sum error to0.39–1.66%; linear-charge interpolation leaves3.74–4.59% error. The0.662mV order spread on that large-sum site still requires state/order characterization. A329-event32.623µV current sums within9–22nV under ideal gate drive, but this is far below the independent10µm-pair thermal scale and does not settle threshold fidelity. Do not declare a physical precision target from this schematic agreement. Gate generation/calibration, noise, readout, state store, PLIF feedback and 5ms service remain architecture gates.

Require a per-corner nonlinear gate-transfer reference before promoting the amplitude injector. Ideal separately measured SS/TT/FF maps keep one actual370-event sum within0.03–1.67% magnitude error, but using the fixed SS map at TT gives2.69–2.82× the target magnitude and at FF gives strongly order-dependent outcomes including sign reversal. This rejects one static gate table over the selected corners. It does not select a specific trim architecture or establish local mismatch tolerance; keep the analog event source provisional and the layout gate closed.

Reject direct sampled-capacitor connection to the injector gate as the current analog weight-cell implementation. The10µm cap suffers large first-event charge sharing; even a100µm pair with5ms hold and adequate programming has2.90% event error and≈2,874.56mm² bare all-PLIF-weight capacitor area. Keep drain-steered continuous gate bias only as an alternate circuit research path: VCM idle clamping reduces neutral switching injection to0.351µV at one SS point, but introduces static current and5ms weight drift of0.77–91% across tested capacitor sizes. Neither result supplies a real corner-calibrated analog weight/reference system or qualifies a full-float synapse. Explore isolation/buffering or a measured low-leak store before large-scale layout.

The existing SKY130 OTA does not yet solve analog gate isolation as a direct shared driver. Its SS/0°C DC follower has millivolt error at low n-gate levels and its loaded off-to-weight n-gate step settles over about7–9µs, far longer than the50.253ns event spacing in the sampled370-event train. Preserve it as a prefetched-buffer research option, subject to measured mux, bias, noise, calibrated transfer and service. Do not add it to the accepted injector or area/power inventory yet.

A 12-bit TT-trimmed shared R-2R source may remain a provisional analog shared-bias candidate: repeated positive and negative event trains preserve the nominal per-event gain deficit at two sampled bias points, while five-event SS/FF tests remain within1.0–3.6%. Do not treat this as a qualified synapse. The ten-event SS deck collapsed numerically; resistors remain ideal and device mismatch, noise, extraction, calibrated float-weight mapping, threshold/readout and closed-loop PLIF are unverified. Proceed to signed float fan-in and cancellation through the physical gate rails.

The denser finite-level map is a useful analog candidate screen but not a complete weight architecture: nearest selection across13 measured widths and five biases yields1.54%/0.53%/2.26% local spike mismatch for final_fuse/enc1/context2. Geometry-to-charge reversals mean continuous monotone width calibration is not supported by these measurements. Keep the candidate provisional until signed actual float fan-in, cancellation/order, negative polarity, state dependence, DAC loading/calibration and PLIF feedback are tested. Do not begin layout.

The measured 104-level geometry/bias library is not sufficient for acceptance. Although its local float32 replay stays within1.41% spike mismatch and its selected event charges sum within0.24% of the chosen float fan-in, the physical shared-bank transient underdelivers by8.8–10.0% and changes0.660mV with event order. This may reflect state-dependent response and/or the emulated bank's inactive MOS loading; the full-fanin deck does not contain a physical R-2R ladder. Keep the event source provisional. Next compare an implementation with one static-sized device pair per synapse and quantify leakage/hold against the shared bank, then include signed threshold/readout feedback. No layout or analog acceptance yet.

Reject the current per-synapse drain-isolation topology: a one-event probe yields +20.871mV for a −0.924mV target and the 50-event probe yields −137.364mV for +1.261mV ideal. The large error is consistent with floating internal drain charge/switch feedthrough, but polarity/topology effects are not excluded. Do not use these isolated results as synapse evidence. Debug precharge and switch-only coupling at one event before longer runs.

Reject the current per-synapse isolation variant for signed accumulation: precharge plus a 4× TG matches one event within1.3% but is26.9% low on the first ten events and overshoots the first fifty by182%; a16× TG is worse over ten events. Retain the shared bank as the better evidenced topology. A1.05× event-width stretch improves its full370-event SS/0°C sum to4.0–5.4% low, but uses the old40.253ns lookup and has0.718mV order spread. Treat it as a calibration lead, not an accepted design. Re-measure its event library at42.266ns before further mapping or architecture acceptance.

## Decision — retain pulse-duration extension on the matched analog map (2026-09-30)

Continue with the shared-bank full-float analog charge mapper: analog gate bias encodes measured weight magnitude, signed event polarity selects the p/n path, and pulse duration extends the lowest charge range. Use the bank-loaded 42.2657ns map and 2.5ns lower duration calibration for current schematics. Keep switched-capacitor charge transfer as the fallback only if the measured injector cannot meet closed-loop spike accuracy, noise, service or area gates.

Evidence supports continued exploration, not acceptance: the 370-event SS/0°C sequence is within1.65% of target in the worst tested order, and the near-threshold 329-event sequence is within1.30%. Same-site deterministic spike decisions are unchanged, but their mapped margins are only4.09–5.77uV versus an existing ~200uV thermal-noise proxy. Do not lay out or scale the synapse as a functioning PLIF until a physical low-disturbance threshold/readout, beta/reset state update, noise/mismatch and held-out spike replay pass. Keep float32 weights and original checkpoint; no QAT or learned quantization.

## Charge-bucket reference decision — 2026-09-30

Do not select the continuous single-ended OTA integrator with a separate zero-charge OTA reference for the low-microvolt PLIF readout. Matched schematic 100-event packet transfer looks symmetric, but balanced residuals are6.8–9.1µV; a0.1mV reference-bias perturbation moves the output by~177µV without controlled startup and~299µV with timed startup feedback. Deck-composition dependence reaches~1.98mV even after physical VCM precharge and switched startup feedback. These are deterministic TT device screens and do not establish statistical mismatch. Preserve the result as evidence; explore a shared differential front end with explicit common-mode and offset control while keeping the analog full-float model. This decision does not reject all charge-domain integration or restore digital/QAT architecture.

## Weighted-event latch decision — 2026-09-30

Keep the clocked MIM latch as a nominal comparator reference, not the selected PLIF readout. A real 329-event signed float-weight sequence and the transistor latch give correct decisions on either side of the trained threshold, but a 4µV diagnostic threshold shift reveals a 0.222µV deterministic boundary crossing, while independent 10µm reset noise is estimated at about192µV RMS at0°C. The learned threshold is still an ideal initialization voltage and there is no physical beta/reset loop. Continue analog shared differential/offset-controlled readout and dense state storage before macro layout. Do not infer a QAT or digital switch from this result.

## State representation direction — 2026-09-30

Do not choose a fixed linear gain and hard clamp for the full-float membrane. On two complete records, an optimistic per-record32–64× range compression plus independent10µm-pair Gaussian reset-noise proxy still changes4.6–4.9% of final-fusion spikes and about20–22% of pixel classes;100× gain makes roughly14% of spikes and more than half of pixels disagree. Higher gain reduces input-referred noise but clips accumulated state. Keep the analog research direction: evaluate a *specific* nonlinear or split-range state representation with shared differential common-mode/offset control, then validate a physical state store and actual weighted-event/reset behavior. This is a research direction, not a selected circuit or evidence for QAT/digital fallback.

The ideal `asinh` state bound improves this software tradeoff but is not a design selection. Its mathematical inverse preserves the zero-noise float trajectory; at about1010× near-zero voltage gain the10µm-pair noise proxy still changes2.31/2.41% of final-fusion spikes and12–13% of pixel classes on two records. It requires a state-dependent signed event transformation absent from the current additive MOS injector, plus physical analog storage, threshold and reset. Explore a realizable split-range/charge-domain mechanism before selecting any nonlinear state macro. No QAT or digital model switch.

Use an eight-range piecewise state-voltage map as the next **physical feasibility target**, not a selected macro. It approximates the ideal compander in a two-frame software noise screen, but would require~4,496× gain span and charge-preserving transitions across segment boundaries. Prove one signed boundary-crossing event and held state in a bounded SKY130 circuit before any macro, area or full-neuron claim. If that physical mechanism fails, return to another analog state representation without changing the trained float model.
The first positive boundary-crossing bench passed only with ideal matched precharge, manual timing and ideal current, while its5ms hold decayed. Therefore the physical gate remains reverse crossing with an actual weighted source, programmable boundary reference, and retention/noise; the conditional positive result cannot select a macro.

The complete eight-range *per-state capacitance bank* is ruled out by the conditional~28.9m² bare-MIM area calculation. Prioritize state-dependent signed event-charge generation on a modest analog store or a shared active integrator. The actual max-fanin float-event demand reaches342mV for one ideal encoded update and5–22 events per examined order exceed the existing16.624mV single-event bank calibration, so the linear direct-MOS event lookup cannot simply be reused unchanged. Physical event subdivision, state sensing/control, reverse range crossing and hold/noise still require evidence; the eight-range map remains a software bound.

The bank-loaded ten-pulse screen makes state-dependent signed balance a required gate for the direct-MOS branch. Equal positive and negative pulses cancel almost exactly at zero stored differential but leave~0.131mV residual at±0.6V. Do not extrapolate a zero-state gate map to biased high-cancellation paths; the next physical source candidate needs state-aware polarity compensation or another differential charge-balancing mechanism.

Manual polarity-specific ideal gate bias restores nominal ten-pulse cancellation to≤0.6µV at the two screened±0.6V states. Keep state-aware polarity correction as a plausible analog research path. It cannot be selected until a physical sensing/programming loop, interpolated state law, actual mixed float weights, noise/mismatch and5ms service are demonstrated.

The actual mixed float-weight physical fan-in now narrows that path: ideal first-segment gain produces a good23.699mV near-threshold sum, but a separate+5.442µV high-cancellation target has up to67.089µV signed error and a shuffled sign reversal. Reusing the equal-pulse state/polarity coefficient across mixed weights only partly helps. Do not select the fixed zero-state MOS lookup or its one-geometry polarity correction as a precise companded injector; require a measured weight/geometry/state calibration and physical controller, then repeat the full signed fan-in and noise/hold gates.

The high-cancellation prefix screen locates~20µV signed error by event120 while the event mix spans all13 geometries and a broad bias range. Treat that as an injector transfer-calibration problem, but keep independent-state noise, retention and population area as parallel feasibility gates. Do not spend layout effort on a locally calibrated injector before the state representation has a credible physical storage and readout path.

The first physical range-switch screen does not clear that target. Ideal boundary-matched extension preparation allows a manually timed positive current event to cross one boundary with0.177mV immediate state error, but the actual precharge source and range detector are absent. A VCM-precharged extension loses~71.5mV of the intended112.5mV state at connection. Even with matched ideal preparation, the event difference decays6.82% over5ms. Keep the eight-range circuit conditional; qualify precharge, reverse crossing, hold compensation and noise before selection or layout.

Prefer testing a **shared fixed boundary reference** for extension precharge before designing per-state tracking buffers. At an exact known segment boundary, the extension target is fixed; a deterministic±1mV reference perturbation moves the positive-crossing final state by about±0.635mV. This is a source-calibration and connection-timing requirement, not proof of feasible distribution or mismatch tolerance. The physical reference/programming circuit and range detector remain absent; this refinement does not accept the macro.

Reject the full-range **per-state switched-capacitance bank** implied by the ideal eight-range voltage map. Holding event charge per model unit fixed would require~933pF per side in its final segment,~683µm square SKY130 MIMs and~28.9m² bare two-cap area for30.976m logical states. The one-boundary TG/MIM bench remains useful for switching physics, but should not be replicated as an eight-range store. Retain the nonlinear software result only as a target for state-dependent event-charge control or shared active integration on a smaller store; neither has passed a physical circuit or model replay with measured errors. Analog full float remains the reference.
## MOS gate storage remains diagnostic — 2026-10-01

A bounded SKY130 TT screen gives3.14–3.84× the MIM capacitance at equal100µm² drawn gate area, but physical-TG off-center 5ms hold drifts3.7–4.6mV and an independent differential pair still has a102–113µV kT/C proxy. Even the optimistic full-state gate-only area at10µm-MIM-pair capacitance is1,613–1,975mm² before layout overhead. Do not select a transistor gate as the resident analog state cell yet. Continue the shared analog refresh/sense path with actual float signed-event preservation, service sizing, noise and PVT as gates. No digital/QAT fallback is authorized.

The immediate refresh follow-up shows why a **simple per-state refresh sweep** should not proceed to layout: the nominal drift implies millions of parallel 1µs refresh channels under an illustrative3µV error bound, and a single exponential leakage calibration misses intermediate states by22–258µV in one TT5ms screen. A smarter analog state representation or physically measured state-dependent correction is required. These conditional probes do not prove every analog state store impossible; the full-float analog path remains active.

Do not lay out the tested two-TG stacked MOS state store. Adding up to a10µm middle-node MIM does not reduce the3.7–4.6mV5ms drift. Ideal-initialized bare storage has zero nominal drift, while the off NFET/PFET attached to it create opposing millivolt leakage. Continue device/bias topology research or a separately justified analog state representation before any macro selection.

Do not choose the tested body-to-state switch bias. Its largest simulated movements occur outside or at the documented device body-bias range, while the in-range single-device cases still move millivolts and have no isolated-well layout. The special SkyWater SRAM pass devices are hard-IP-restricted, so their name is not grounds for a custom analog switch claim. No MOS state-store macro is selected; module/layout/full-chip readiness remains false.

The official public SKY1305V-gate NFET/PFET models give essentially the same sampled MOS state drift as the installed1.8V TG at the tested TT points. Do not select a simple thick-oxide substitution for layout. Continue to seek a physically supported state representation that passes signed update, 5ms retention, noise and population service together; original full-float analog remains the target.

The new full-float two-frame replay demotes the **linear small-MOS state cell under independent reset noise**: a113µV kT/C-scale proxy plus measured TT hold drift gives13.7% final-fusion spike mismatch and mean IoU~0.119 against float~0.20. The ideal eight-range map under the same proxy has~2.9% spike mismatch and near-baseline selected-frame IoU, but its physical nonlinear update/sense is absent. Keep the original full-float analog direction and research a state-dependent charge/sense circuit; do not lay out either candidate as a completed PLIF cell. The software results do not prove dataset accuracy or physical noise yield.


2026-10-02: Keep the complementary active-bucket latch as an experimental readout candidate only. Minimum legal clocked PMOS input assistance resolves the selected float margin at TT and FF, but both assisted and unassisted versions fail SS; an ideal−5µV threshold perturbation repairs the selected SS case while FF required a positive correction. This corner dependence favors a physically calibrated differential auto-zero/trim study over selecting a fixed input-pair size. No trim topology or readout is accepted, and layout remains gated.


2026-10-02: The float-margin histogram invalidates any claim that a ±5µV single-site latch correction is sufficient. Under the provisional SKY130 mapping, about0.20% of 1.97M sampled final-fusion states are within1µV of threshold, and the closest0.1% of positive decisions have a0.264µV margin. Require measured offset/noise and replay across these margins before layout; do not select the current latch or call the selected-site pass representative.


The independent Gaussian margin calculation is only a proposed characterization grid:1µV RMS maps to0.077% per-decision flips,5µV to0.375%. Do not translate these directly into pixel accuracy; run the measured-error full-float replay once physical noise/offset waveforms are available.


2026-10-02: Select the ideal8-segment nonlinear state map as the next physical analog research branch because it substantially improves full-float decision/pixel stability under injected threshold noise on two complete frames. Keep the active differential bucket as the event-charge reference while replacing ideal weight/state mapping with a physical range-aware store/update. This is a direction for experiments only; no nonlinear cell, 5ms hold, mismatch/noise, or layout is accepted. No quantized model or digital fallback is introduced.

2026-10-02 readout trim decision status: complementary trim is the current schematic candidate based on the physical MIM/latch q0.1 margin screen. VCM-referenced single-DAC trim failed that screen. This is an experiment direction, not an accepted architecture decision; extraction/matching, calibration, noise, PVT and full-float replay still decide whether this readout survives.

2026-10-02: Resolve the earlier single-resistor vs flattened-network mismatch by mapping flat Magic resistor devices to the exact size-specific PCell wrapper stored in the source PCell geometry. The mapped series pair is2.450602kΩ (−2.68% nominal) with unique LVS; raw generic netlist is retained. This is the extraction convention for the current SKY130 PCell probe, pending array-level validation.

2026-10-02: Promote the mapped three-tap resistor string as a reusable test primitive only. Its IN/TAP1/TAP2/OUT pins are physically connected in flat extraction, its three PCell segments each measure1.225298kΩ, and DRC/LVS/GDS pass. It does not accept the R-2R trim DAC; loaded transfer and a physical switched branch must pass first.

2026-10-02: Continue the trim DAC along the physical resistor path: extracted tapped string meets the illustrative10MΩ loading screen, and the2-bit R-2R schematic is monotonic across four codes but has unequal steps. Next integrate an extracted SKY130 transmission-gate cell and characterize multi-code transfer before array scaling.


2026-10-02: Keep the extracted SKY130 TG as a reusable primitive only. It is a2.11×7.59µm DRC/LVS-clean primitive, with ten extracted coupling capacitances and monotonic2-bit ladder behavior over TT/SS/FF. Strongly signal-dependent on-resistance and0.10–0.18mV five-millisecond MIM hold drift make it unsuitable as an accepted precision state switch. Do not use the legacy24-bit DAC/membrane-code candidate to define analog model precision. Continue with the active full-float differential charge bucket and resolve state storage/readout, mismatch/noise and service before full-chip layout.

2026-10-02: Keep the100×10µm SKY130 MOS gate-cap differential pair as the current state-store experiment lead because its measured SS/TT/FF retention plus ideal piecewise8 mapping gives about96.7% pixel agreement and≈0.49% final-fuse spike mismatch on two frames. Reject the20×2µm pair for now: its≈0.30m² bare pair area saves area but drops pixel agreement to≈91.9%. This does not accept the100×10µm macro; its corrected zero-state capacitance is14.45pF/rail at VCM=0.9V, its bare pair area is≈0.76m², and event drive, readout, noise/mismatch, array service and full inference remain unqualified. Correct the stale grounded-channel capacitance/noise reports before using those figures further.


CMFB actuator sizing screen at FF/85°C with the extracted TG: the stock0.42/10µm PMOS assist leaves the final output common mode near0.423V; increasing to2/1µm moves it only to0.507V, and2/0.15µm reaches0.802V. The last case still ends with about−0.205mV differential error on the shuffled high-cancellation sequence. This actuator-only tweak improves common mode but corrupts the useful differential state by orders of magnitude beyond full-float margin. Reject sizing alone as the fix; the active path needs a better matched differential integrator and common-mode servo topology before layout.


2026-10-02: Keep shared reset bridge timing as a sequencing improvement only: open the rail bridge before releasing the VCM clamps, which suppresses deterministic differential kick in the extracted transient. Do not claim correlated-noise cancellation; bridge release still samples a differential degree of freedom. The 24µV full-float noise replay is promising with piecewise8/gain100–10000, but that encoding and noise source are idealized. Continue with physical float-weight fan-in and measured injector error before macro selection.


2026-10-02: The extracted 100×10µm MOS state bank plus present 4.4µs pulse injector fails the full-float high-cancellation fan-in (55.2µV error and wrong sign); tested behavioral feedback/calibration did not improve it. Keep the state pair as a characterized layout primitive, but shift active injection experiments to the differential switched-capacitor charge bucket. No macro or full-chip architecture accepted.

2026-10-03: Reject open-loop MOS pulse/current injection as the selected full-float accumulator. Extracted-layout state-bank simulation with a42.2657ns transfer table gives a small order spread on one322-event high-cancellation trace, but a−75nV signed residual overwhelms the+7.49nV float target; measured±50µV state dependence also changes event gain by several percent and can reverse low-bias events. The state bank and injector remain characterized primitives. Continue with a closed-loop fully differential OTA-C integrator and internal common-mode feedback; this is still an experimental direction, not an accepted architecture.

2026-10-03 decision: direct-current OTA-C bench is currently invalid as a circuit result (transient aborts, and ngspice exit status is misleading); common-mode servo polarity variants both fail the 0.9V hold target. Retain the analog/full-float objective, but do not advance the integrated OTA-C to layout until a no-event 5ms simulation holds its operating point at TT/SS/FF. Reuse only separately qualified MIM/TG layout evidence.

2026-10-03 follow-up: reject OTA input reversal because its TT common level is about1.4mV. Correct polarity has approximately0.898V DC OP, but transient startup timestep-underflows at4ns even with50pF compensation. Treat the current integrator bench as failed; next establish OTA stability with/without the large MIM load and do not advance integrated layout until this is fixed.

2026-10-03 original-float replay decision: reject the two-independent-OTA differential bucket and present single-ended fixed-bias variant for layout. Both fail the322-event float replay; one-event transfer alone is not sufficient. Continue analog path by finding a stable single-ended OTA bias/operating point and passing actual high-cancellation event order before integration/layout.

2026-10-03 single-ended OTA-C bias follow-up: retain the full-float analog direction, but reject this OTA-C candidate for neuron layout. Corrected single-ended stimulus normalization and near-VCM summing-node operation do not prevent large baseline-corrected sequence error or event-order dependence. Continue with a state-aware charge correction or a different closed-loop injection method; reuse only individually qualified MIM/TG physical cells until event-order, PVT, retention and readout checks pass.

2026-10-03 OTA-C DC-return decision: reject the present single-ended OTA-C macro for layout. A lower return resistor gives a virtual-ground point but fails the original-float event sequence; a higher return resistor rails in DC. Fixed positive/negative gain trim and event-gated OTA are not qualified. Continue analog/full-float work with autozero/common-mode correction or another state-aware charge-transfer topology.

2026-10-03: reject the dual same-sign passive reservoir for neuron layout. Even with extracted MIM and TG cells, balanced switch clocks, and the original float fan-in, update error is−5.21µV to−66.88µV across TT event orders against a+0.749µV target; SS shuffle misses by−35.37µV. The 5ms hold is stable only in this initialized test. Continue analog/full-float exploration with a physically charge-balanced packet and dummy-switch cancellation before layout.

2026-10-03: Keep fixed-width, full-float weight-amplitude MOS injection as a provisional event-source candidate. Reject the prior pulse-duration direction for this fan-in: its measured transfer was6.7–33x high. Do not accept a global TT map: it misses36% at SS/27°C and65% at FF/27°C. Corner/temperature-specific isolated-event maps recover the two sampled original-float sequences within2.2% across SS/0°C, SS/27°C, FF/27°C and FF/85°C, but depend on ideal gate voltages. No architecture/layout acceptance until a physical analog weight hold/driver and calibration scheme reproduces that map with noise, mismatch and state-dependence characterized.

2026-10-03 static geometry weight follow-up: the segmented runner had been restarting each chunk at the DC operating point; its apparent 23% full-sequence error is invalid as state-carry evidence. Correct UIC starts exposed rapid off-state loading in the direct geometry bank: with a 100µm state cap the differential moved 2.90mV during the 90ns before the first event. A per-branch series NMOS access switch reduced that pre-event change to 34nV, and a polarity-reversed, 5× widened single-event case delivered +0.695mV against +0.683mV target (1.81% error) at SS/0°C. This is one isolated event only: the 20-event fan-in deck fails because the largest geometry-scaled MOS exceeds the PDK model range, and an attempted 5-finger instance aborts at transient startup. Do not treat the geometry bank as an accepted accumulator. Corrected scripts now require the ngspice completion marker and reject transient aborts, and reports record requested initial state voltages. Next measure an in-range isolated-access transfer grid and off-state retention, then replay ordered float fan-in only if both pass; integrated layout remains gated.

Follow-up qualification boundary (2026-10-03): with in-range geometry widths and a PEX-style series transmission gate, the one-event sweep at headroom0.2–0.9 delivers only34–20% of the float event charge (66–80% low). Widening the transmission gate from10 to40µm improves one-event charge from0.115mV to0.357mV against0.683mV, while common mode shifts up to2.7mV. A prior 5× raw MOS width case matched one event but exceeded the PDK geometry range in the full-fanin deck; reject that apparent scaling solution. Instantiating all360 branches was killed by the bounded Docker memory limit, and a33-branch attempt hit timestep underflow at the event edge. These are simulator/bench limits plus an actual underdriven event; no full sequence or integrated layout passes. Next proceed with a convergent isolated-switch driver or the shared calibrated injector physical weight-source build, preserving the analog full-float reference.

Series-isolated pulse-width control check (2026-10-03): the first float event was sampled1ns after the pulse at10.5/30/52.5ns. Measured differential increments were0.230/0.217/0.202mV against+0.683mV target (−66.4/−68.3/−70.4%); common mode shifted+0.69mV. This confirms the in-range underdrive is not caused by the former100ns observation delay, and extending pulse width makes transfer slightly worse. Preserve full-float event source and investigate a differently biased current injector or shared physical DAC path; do not layout the isolated geometry cell.

2026-10-03: the shared analog 12-bit R-2R bias ladder (not a learned-weight quantizer) with PDK TGs retains monotonic DC codes, but TT-trimmed event delivery is 2.3–3.2% low at TT, 4.6–6.0% low at SS, and 1.4–2.0% low at FF on the tested 0.85/0.90/0.95 rails. Resistors are still ideal in this event bench; no physical DAC acceptance. Require physical resistor-ratio layout/extraction and analog per-corner calibration before weight/event replay.

2026-10-03: A routed 2-bit SKY130 resistor-only R-2R slice now passes Magic DRC0 and unique Netgen LVS; GDS exists. The extracted passive ladder has <0.005% code-step variation with ideal rails, but the existing 1um/2um physical TG has 21–37% variation through TT/SS/FF. Schematic switch multiplicity needs about1000x to reach the best sampled 1.32% bound; no such high-drive layout has been qualified. Continue physical switch-array build and integration. Do not promote the current DAC or neuron layout.

2026-10-04 modeling decision — use a reduced-order MIM model for millisecond system transients, keep full SKY130 MIM for device-level checks. The PDK cell has nominal C=9.52fF for w=l=2µm and internal series resistance r1+r2≈0.0976Ω, giving τ≈0.93fs. That ESR is below the physical time scales of the 5ms neuron service by over12 orders of magnitude. In the standard sub-fs RC expansion, ngspice shows common-mode state drift with every external rail at0.9V; native C-R-R expansion reproduces it, while direct nominal C removes it. Therefore system-level 5ms PEX transient benches may replace the cell’s internal ESR/subnodes with the same nominal capacitance while preserving extracted interconnect parasitics and selector devices. This is a justified reduced-order simulation model, not a modified production PDK, a measured leakage assumption, or a claim that the unmodified device model passes. Preserve separate short-time PDK element/primitive checks and keep noise/mismatch, full-float accuracy and service integration gates open.
## Compact body-tied state switch remains a probe — 2026-10-05

Do not replicate the isolated-well TG as the30.976M resident-state cell. Its local Magic DRC0/Netgen LVS and parked-source PEX hold are physical controls; the source bus must not be assumed to track every changing state. On a fixed0.9V shared bus, the TT/27°C cell with both PDK well-diode proxies loses36.6% of a ±1mV differential span and58.9% of a ±300mV span over5ms, even with DNW1.2V balancing common-mode drift. Equal ideal early/late event charges also retain different final increments (0.315/0.453mV). The unshared differential switch footprint extrapolates to6,329mm² before MIM and routing. These are diagnostic simulations with no extracted junction geometry or foundry signoff. Keep the original full-float SNN contract and investigate an analog state store with true isolation or a physically timed state read/restore cycle, then check service and model accuracy before selecting a macro.

The passive two-TG guard branch is not selected either:10fF raises the early/late ratio only to0.773, and100fF lowers it to0.718, with an unshared TG footprint of12,658mm² before capacitors. The current30µs sampled readout separately needs185,856 zero-overhead parallel channels and4,751mm² of sample-bank bounding boxes for one5ms visit to all residents. Do not combine these unqualified pieces into a claimed full chip. Prioritize a truly integrated state write/read/restore circuit and a faster, lower-area sensing method before physical population assembly.

## GF180 assembled reader: physical checks, settling and noise — 2026-10-06

Built the seven-MOS routed OTA core and grounded7/22um MIM primitives, then combined OTA, long-gate reset pair, reference diode and both caps into one10MOS/2MIM reader. Magic DRC0, Netgen unique LVS, no extraction feedback, real GDS; reader138x79.24um (10,935.12um2),50 capacitance entries. Fixed0.8V schematic bias had approximately28x corner current spread; a20uA external current into a physical bias diode gives approximately115..118uA total reader current. External current/reference supplies and phase sources are still ideal. Direct physical Miller capacitance is used without a provisional compensation resistor. MIM primitives now ground substrate explicitly; floating-VSUBS predecessor runs are superseded.

Combined capacitance-extracted read screens complete at typical27/FF85/SS0 and typical2mV offset. Gain from held state span to inverted output is0.42184/0.42814/0.41350 (near-zero typical0.42436). Maximum100ns-versus900ns output differences are0.761/3.338/0.383uV, while state is driven to approximately1.6493V and erased. Zero read output is1.6541..1.6547V and subsequent access switch-off changes output by millivolts. No physical reference subtraction, restore, encoder or complete neuron result is implied.

A naive read-phase DC operating point saturates output at0.021V and is invalid for transient noise referral. A separate bench freezes OUT/SUM to completed transient zero-state voltages using ideal1e18H AC-open auxiliary inductors, then characterizes the stationary small-signal noise. Output RMS over10Hz..100MHz is268.16/285.74/261.62uV; typical reset-on noise297.72uV. These are stationary phase screens, not sampled state error or covariance. Secant gain referral gives633..667uV linear input proxies, not decoder/model acceptance. Noise-current input referral is in amperes because the AC probe is a current source.

One/four separately extracted22x22um MIM output loads reduce typical output noise to235.80/201.83uV but increase100ns settling differences to33.08uV/21.58mV; four-load500ns difference is2.47uV. Increased output load alone is not selected as the accuracy solution. Ideal spectral boxcar/difference probes remain unimplemented; overlapping acquisition windows and same-phase noise correlation cannot establish distinct pre/post sampling cancellation. Next prototype physical averaging/correlated sensing and analog restore/nonlinear encoding; measure switched noise, covariance, mismatch, qualified gate leakage, resistance extraction, original-float accuracy, array density and complete5ms service. Full-float analog goal active, no QAT/digital fallback or full-chip completion. All launched runs terminal. Next build actual shared offset storage/subtraction and sampled analog restore, then nonlinear encoding and array-packed sensor comparison.
